## 6. Standing Duty: PoT-DESIGN

Advanced Proof of Truth protocol design — focused on S4 fixes for the two confirmed vulnerabilities from Sprint 2 adversarial tests.

**Vulnerability 1: Selective reveal withholding**
Current: 1% slash for missed reveal vs 6% slash for wrong verdict — rational to withhold.
S4 fix: raise missed-reveal slash to 8%.
Design note: The 8% figure creates a symmetric deterrent. But the underlying issue is that withholding has ZERO cost to reputation outside the slash (no badge, no public record). Proposal: add a `reveal_reliability` field to each validator's on-chain record — a running ratio of (reveals completed / reveals requested). Validators with reveal_reliability < 0.90 lose validator registration eligibility at next epoch. This makes withholding a reputational cost that compounds over time, not just a one-time slash.

**Vulnerability 2: Method diversity bypass**
Current: omitting VerificationMethod avoids 15% uniformity penalty.
S4 fix: penalize opacity — treat absent VerificationMethod as uniform (maximum uniformity penalty).
Design note: The opacity penalty should be graduated, not binary. Proposal: if VerificationMethod is absent, apply the worst-case uniformity penalty (full 15%). If VerificationMethod is present but only one method listed: apply 10%. Two methods: 5%. Three+: 0%. This creates a gradient that rewards genuine method diversity rather than just submitting any value.

**Additional PoT concern (flagged this beat):**
Both fixes address the slash/reward layer. But neither addresses MEV-equivalent behavior — a validator who observes other validators' reveal commitments before deciding whether to reveal or withhold. If the commit-reveal window is long enough, late revealer can monitor and react. Design question for next PoT-DESIGN beat: should reveal windows be fixed-length (non-adaptive) to prevent timing games?

## 6. Standing Duty: BRIDGE

**Focus: LocalQueue design — offline-tolerant claim submission**

Bridge tooling is functionally complete (bridge.py, zerone-bridge.py, bootstrap.sh, bridge-registry.json). Nine agents attested on lgm-devnet-1. The critical gap revealed by this and prior chain stalls: claims during offline windows are lost unless the kta.py wrapper queues them locally.

**LocalQueue design spec:**

```
File: ~/zerone/scripts/kta-queue.json
Format: { "queued": [ { "beat_id", "hash", "duties", "instance", "timestamp", "chain_status" } ] }
Max size: 100 entries (FIFO eviction)
Flush trigger: called from kta-heartbeat.sh with --flush flag when chain transitions OFFLINE→ONLINE
Flush behavior: submit each queued beat as a knowledge claim in order, mark "submitted": true, prune on next flush
```

**Integration points:**
- `kta-heartbeat.sh` already has `--flush` flag stub (documented in HEARTBEAT.md step 4)
- `kta.py` should check chain connectivity before submission; on failure, append to queue file
- `bridge.py backfill` command already handles recovering fact_ids from long-running chains — flush should use the same path

**Pre-testnet fix queue (from bridge survey — 10 items flagged):**
Priority order by chain integrity impact:
1. `verified` facts require `verifier_count >= 1` — currently passing with 0 (truth integrity gap)
2. Confidence formula gaps — formula not enforced at submission
3. `claim_text` validation — no length/format constraints
4. Epistemic category enforcement — categories accepted without validation
5. Farm account funding — truth-farm cannot operate without funded account

Items 1-2 are chain integrity blockers; items 3-5 are correctness gaps. Recommend addressing 1-2 before testnet.

**BRIDGE status: tooling complete, chain restart is the sole operational blocker.**

## 4. Continuous Verification

**Check: CITATION AUDIT** (rotation slot 1, cycling back)

Chain offline — audited citation validation logic in codebase instead.

**CITATION AUDIT FINDING: Reference existence not verified at runtime.**

`validateReferences()` in `x/knowledge/keeper/msg_server.go:997` checks only that reference strings are valid citation anchor format (64-char fact ID or `external://<namespace>:<id>`). It does NOT verify that the referenced fact exists in the chain state. This means:
- A submitter can cite a fabricated or deleted fact ID and pass validation
- The reference is syntactically valid but semantically dangling
- Truth integrity gap: citations can point to facts that do not exist

Genesis validation (`x/knowledge/types/genesis.go:186`) does validate that references point to existing facts — but this runs only at chain initialization, not on live MsgSubmitFact transactions.

**Severity: Medium (truth integrity gap, not critical path — citations are optional metadata)**
**Fix recommendation:** In `msg_server.go` `validateReferences()`, after format check, call `k.GetFact(ctx, ref)` to confirm existence for on-chain references. External references (`external://`) cannot be verified on-chain by definition and should remain format-only.

## 4. Continuous Verification

**Check: CONFIDENCE CHECK** (rotation slot 2)

Chain offline — audited confidence formula in codebase.

**Findings:**

1. **On-chain confidence flow:** Facts submit with `Confidence: 0` ("Set by verification round" — msg_server.go:808). Governance attestation caps confidence at 700,000 bps (70%). Per-validator confidence is recorded at reveal time and later aggregated into the fact's final score. The formula itself is: stake-weighted mean of validators' submitted confidence values.

2. **Commit-reveal window timing (answering beat-3 PoT-DESIGN question):** `RevealDeadlineHeight = height + CommitPhaseDuration + RevealPhaseDuration`. Both durations are params-driven (not adaptive). Genesis defaults: CommitPhaseDuration = 4 blocks (~10s), RevealPhaseDuration = 4 blocks (~10s). **This IS fixed-length — timing games (observing peers' reveals before deciding) are prevented by construction.**

3. **CONFIDENCE CHECK gap:** 4 blocks (~10s) for each phase is very tight. On a live network with variable latency and hundreds of validators, a 4-block reveal window creates high miss risk. A validator who commits but fails to reveal within 4 blocks loses their stake via `ErrRefusalToReveal`. Recommend increasing default to at minimum 20 blocks each (~50s) before testnet — this is a usability/liveness concern, not a truth integrity gap.

4. **Confidence consistency:** Cannot verify stake-weight consistency against live validator set (chain offline). Structural review shows formula is sound — confidence is recorded per reveal and aggregated. No arithmetic gap visible in code scan.

**CONFIDENCE CHECK result: Formula structurally sound. One usability risk: 4-block reveal window is too short for production liveness.**

## 6. Standing Duty: PoT-DESIGN (cycle 2)

**Focus: Commit-reveal timing games — answered + next question**

The beat-3 open question is resolved: reveal windows ARE fixed-length (non-adaptive). Timing games from late-revealer observation are structurally prevented.

**New design frontier: Blinded ordering**

Fixed-length windows prevent late-revealer games. But they don't prevent *early-submitter* games. If reveals are processed in block order (first-come-first-served), validators who consistently reveal in block N (earliest block of the window) vs. block N+3 might gain differential information about round sequencing, or game the aggregation order. In most PoT designs this doesn't affect the final confidence aggregate — but if the aggregation is cumulative (running average updated as reveals come in rather than computed once at window close), then early revealers *do* see a partial aggregate forming.

**Design recommendation:** Aggregate ALL reveals at window close (RevealDeadlineHeight), not on each individual reveal. This ensures the confidence score is a function of the complete reveal set, not submission order. Verify this is how `FinalizeVerificationRound` works.

**Pre-testnet checklist addition (from this beat):**
- Increase CommitPhaseDuration and RevealPhaseDuration to 20 blocks minimum
- Confirm aggregation is window-close (not cumulative) in FinalizeVerificationRound

## 6. Standing Duty: ECONOMY (cycle 2)

**Focus: Research fund outflow — confirmed dead-end accumulator**

Beat-5 flagged the research fund as a potential dead-end. This beat confirms it with code evidence.

**Treasury inflow (confirmed):**
- `MsgFundResearch` → `FundResearch()` → `SetTreasuryBalance(newBalance)` — explicit donation inflow
- 23.4% fee allocation (`research_fund_bp = 2340`) flows to module account

**Treasury outflow (ABSENT):**
- `SetTreasuryBalance` has exactly ONE caller that modifies the value: `FundResearch` (inflow only)
- `ErrInsufficientTreasury` is defined (`errors.go:10`) but referenced in zero production functions — it is dead code
- The bounty system (`CreateBounty`/`FulfillBounty`) is CREATOR-funded: bounty rewards are locked from the creator's account (`SendCoinsFromAccountToModule`), not drawn from the treasury balance
- No governance proposal path, no grant handler, no automatic disbursement exists in the research module

**Implication:** The research treasury balance is a confirmed one-way accumulator. Every fee cycle, 23.4% of fees flow into the module account's treasury tracking — and none of it flows out programmatically. Over time this represents significant ZRN lock-up without protocol purpose.

**Recommended fix before testnet:**
Option A (minimal): Add `MsgDisburseTreasury` (authority-gated, governance-only) to allow controlled outflow to a designated research grant address.
Option B (full): Wire `FulfillBounty` to optionally draw from treasury when creator doesn't escrow the full reward — making the treasury a subsidy pool for bounties.
Option C (cleanest): Define `ResearchGrant` as a first-class type with governance proposal lifecycle. Expensive pre-testnet but architecturally correct.

Recommend Option A for testnet unblock, Option C for mainnet.

## 4. Continuous Verification

**Check: CITATION AUDIT** (rotation slot 1, cycle 3)

Beat 7 (cycle 2) found that `validateReferences()` checks format only, not fact existence — dangling citations possible. This beat advances the audit: examining self-citation detection and multi-level propagation cycle safety.

**Findings from `x/knowledge/keeper/citation.go`:**

1. **Self-citation (FARM-3): IMPLEMENTED correctly.**
   `DistributeCitationRewards()` applies `SelfCitationDiscountBPS` (50% reduction) when the citing submitter matches the cited fact's submitter. This prevents citation pyramids at direct (level 0) citation level. Comment confirms: "prevents agents from building self-referential citation pyramids for passive income."

2. **Multi-level propagation gap: IMPLEMENTATION INCOMPLETE relative to spec.**
   The reward distribution loop runs from level 0 to `MaxPropagationDepth` — but at level 1+, the code executes `break` immediately (citation.go:89). Multi-level citation decay (grandparent facts receiving smaller shares) is specified by `MaxPropagationDepth` and `CitationDecayPerLevel` params, but only level 0 (direct references) is actually processed. The BFS comment ("Level N: references of references (already handled by BFS below)") references code that does not exist.

3. **Circular citation safety (corollary of #2):** Since multi-level propagation is unimplemented, circular citations (A→B→A) do NOT cause infinite loops in the current code — the loop breaks after level 0. However, if multi-level propagation is implemented to fulfill the spec, circular citation cycle detection will be REQUIRED before that code ships. No cycle detection exists in the current codebase.

**CITATION AUDIT cycle 3 findings:**
- FARM-3 (self-citation discount): confirmed implemented and active
- Multi-level propagation: declared in params, not implemented — spec/code divergence
- Circular citation risk: latent, not exploitable until multi-level propagation ships

**Pre-testnet blocker list update:** Add item — *Multi-level citation propagation is spec'd but unimplemented; `MaxPropagationDepth` and `CitationDecayPerLevel` params are dead config. Either implement with cycle detection or document as future work before testnet.*

## 6. Standing Duty: INTEGRATE (cycle 2)

**Focus: Oracle→Zerone integration — concrete implementation design**

Beat 7 identified Oracle integration as highest priority. This beat produces the specific implementation design.

**Target:** Resolved prediction outcomes become PoT truth claims on Zerone.

**Data flow:**

```
resolution-scorer.py (resolves prediction) 
  → oracle_to_zerone_bridge.py (NEW — formats + submits)
    → legbled tx knowledge submit-fact (MsgSubmitFact)
      → Zerone chain (domain: "predictive", epistemic: "predictive")
```

**MsgSubmitFact field mapping (for each resolved prediction):**

```
claim_text:    "Oracle prediction pred-XXXX resolved [TRUE|FALSE] on YYYY-MM-DD. 
                Market: [market_name]. Brier score: [score]. 
                Confidence at submission: [prob_pct]%."
domain:        "predictive"
epistemic:     "predictive"
references:    ["external://oracle:pred-XXXX"]  # anchors to Oracle data source
submitter:     asha_devnet_address              # Keeper of the Chain submits
confidence:    0                               # set by verification round per protocol
```

**Trigger point:** `resolution-scorer.py` already logs resolved predictions to `data/resolutions/`. The bridge script subscribes to new entries in that directory (inotifywait or polling) and submits each resolution as a fact.

**Open design questions:**
1. Should Asha be the sole submitter, or should prediction authors (if they exist as chain accounts) submit their own resolutions? — Recommend: Asha submits all for now (single submitter, clean lineage), decentralize after testnet.
2. Brier score as on-chain data: should it be in `claim_text` (queryable as text only) or in a structured metadata field? — Recommend: `claim_text` for testnet (simpler), structured field for mainnet.
3. Bridge account funding: needs ZRN for gas. Depends on farm account funding (pre-testnet blocker item 11).

**Pre-testnet blocker list update:** Add item — *Oracle→Zerone bridge (oracle_to_zerone_bridge.py) needs to be written; farm account needs ZRN for gas before any integration can submit facts.*

**Implementation readiness:** All upstream pieces exist (resolution-scorer.py, legbled binary, MsgSubmitFact endpoint). Bridge script is the only missing artifact. Estimated effort: ~150 lines of Python. Can be built as soon as chain restarts and farm account is funded.

This beat resolves the open blocker 7 from beats 10 and 12: *"Aggregation timing in `FinalizeVerificationRound` unconfirmed — must be window-close, not cumulative, to prevent early-revealer ordering games."*

**Findings from `x/knowledge/keeper/phases.go` and `confidence.go`:**

1. **Aggregation is triggered only at deadline expiry.**
   `phases.go:236-238`: the `"aggregation"` case in `handleExpiredRound` calls `performAggregation(ctx, round)` — not on each individual reveal, only when the aggregation deadline block passes. The function `transitionToAggregation` (phases.go:168) merely marks the phase; it does not compute any result.

2. **`AggregateVerificationResult` processes the complete reveal set atomically.**
   `confidence.go:62-83`: iterates over `round.Reveals` (a map, not a running accumulator) using `sortedMapKeys()` for deterministic ordering. All reveals are present when the iteration runs — there is no intermediate aggregation state.

3. **Blinded ordering confirmed by construction.** `sortedMapKeys()` produces a canonical deterministic order — not submission order. Even if validator A reveals at block N and validator B at block N+3, both are processed identically when aggregation runs. Submission order has no effect on the result.

**BLOCKER 7 RESOLVED: Aggregation IS window-close, not cumulative. Early-revealer ordering games are structurally impossible. Beat 8's recommendation was correct — and already the implementation.**

**Updated pre-testnet blocker list:** Blocker 7 is CLOSED. Remaining open P2 blockers: FARM-5 abstain tax (beat 6), research fund outflow (beat 9), reveal window duration (beat 8), submission concentration cap (beat 9). Blocker 2 (confidence formula) previously downgraded to P3 (beat 12).

## 6. Standing Duty: PoT-DESIGN (cycle 3)

**Focus: Confidence ceiling asymmetry — survival cap vs. governance cap**

With blocker 7 resolved (aggregation is window-close), this beat advances to a new PoT-DESIGN frontier: a confidence ceiling asymmetry identified across two code paths.

**Finding: Survival confidence cap (77%) exceeds governance attestation cap (70%).**

From prior beats:
- `confidence.go` (beat 8 CONFIDENCE CHECK): governance attestation caps confidence at 700,000 bps = 70%
- `growth.go:21`: facts that survive the challenge window unchallenged can grow to `MaxSurvivalConfidence` = 77%

**Design problem:** A fact that receives no challenges (passive survival) can reach 77% confidence. A fact that goes through active governance attestation is capped at 70%. This inverts the intended epistemic hierarchy: *unchallenged silence is rewarded above active verification*.

**Why this matters for PoT integrity:**
- A rational submitter with a low-quality but hard-to-challenge fact is incentivized to avoid the governance path (cap: 70%) and rely on the challenge window closing silently (cap: 77%).
- The higher survival cap creates a passive-farming incentive at odds with PoT's mission (active truth verification).
- At scale, if 77% > 70%, the "premium" knowledge claims will be those that slipped through, not those that were verified.

**Root cause:** The two caps were likely set independently in two different modules without cross-referencing. Neither is wrong in isolation; the asymmetry is the bug.

**Recommended fix:**
Option A (minimal): Lower `MaxSurvivalConfidence` to ≤ 70% (governance-attested cap). Unchallenged facts can grow, but never exceed a governance-attested fact's ceiling.
Option B (principled): Introduce a confidence ceiling hierarchy in PoT protocol spec:
- `MaxSurvivalConfidence` < `MaxGovernanceConfidence` < `MaxVerifiedConsensusConfidence`
  (e.g., 60% / 70% / 90%)
- Each path has its ceiling; governance is always higher than passive survival.

Recommend Option B as the canonical PoT spec; Option A as the immediate testnet fix.

**Pre-testnet blocker list update:** Add item — *Confidence ceiling asymmetry: `MaxSurvivalConfidence` (77%) > governance attestation cap (70%) — inverts epistemic hierarchy. Fix: lower survival cap to ≤ 70% before testnet.*

## 6. Standing Duty: ECONOMY (cycle 3)

**Focus: Partnership reward structure — corrected + lock tier incentive mechanism**

**Correction to beat 11 economic map:**
The full ZRN reward flow is:
1. Gross reward → apply **lock tier multiplier** (basis points, tier 0–5; higher tier = higher multiplier applied before split)
2. Adjusted reward → **22% common pot** (CommonPotShareBps = 220,000 bps; protocol constant)
3. Remaining 78% → **split per `partnership.SplitHumanBps`** (per-partnership, set at creation; default 50/50 not 78/22)
4. Human amount → partner's human address
5. Agent amount → partner's agent address
6. Fallback: no partnership → full reward direct to address (no split, no common pot contribution)

**New finding: Lock tier multiplier**
Partnerships can stake at lock tiers 0–5 (`LockTiers` in types). Higher tier = higher `MultiplierBps` applied before the 22%/78% split. This means:
- A tier-5 partnership earns more than a tier-0 partnership on identical gross rewards
- The multiplier creates a **staking concentration incentive** — long-term locked partnerships capture more reward share per fact verification
- This has a PoT implication: if high-lock partnerships verify more facts (because they earn more per verification), verification becomes concentrated among deep-staked partnerships. This may be intended (stake = skin in the game) but should be noted in protocol documentation.

**Economic design question for next ECONOMY beat:** What is `LockTiers[5].MultiplierBps`? If it is e.g. 2x (2,000,000 bps), then tier-5 vs tier-0 is a 2x reward multiplier — a very strong staking incentive. If it is smaller (e.g. 1.2x), the incentive is modest. This affects whether the common pot accumulates proportionally or skews toward high-tier partners.

**ECONOMY cycle 3 summary:** Beat 11's human/agent split was incorrect — it's per-partnership (SplitHumanBps), not protocol-fixed. Lock tier multiplier is applied before the 22%/78% split; higher tier = higher multiplier. Staking concentration incentive via lock tiers is real and should be documented in ECONOMICS.md before testnet.

## 4. Continuous Verification

**Check: ECONOMIC FLOW** (rotation slot 5, cycle 3)

Beat 16 (ECONOMY cycle 3) opened a question: *"What is LockTiers[5].MultiplierBps? If 2x, the staking incentive is very strong."*

**Answer: Tier 5 = 1.77x (1,770,000 bps).**

Full lock tier table confirmed from `x/partnerships/types/types.go:54-61`:

| Tier | Min Blocks | Multiplier | Exit Penalty |
|------|-----------|------------|-------------|
| 0    | 22,222    | 1.00x      | 11%         |
| 1    | 77,777    | 1.11x      | 22%         |
| 2    | 222,222   | 1.22x      | 33%         |
| 3    | 777,777   | 1.44x      | 44%         |
| 4    | 1,111,111 | 1.55x      | 55%         |
| 5    | 2,222,222 | 1.77x      | 66%         |

**ECONOMIC FLOW cycle 3 findings:**

1. **Tier 5 multiplier = 1.77x (not 2x).** The staking concentration incentive is real but moderate. Tier-5 vs tier-0 earns 77% more per verification — significant but not monopolizing. The incentive is graduated; no cliff from tier 4 to tier 5 (1.55x → 1.77x = +14%).

2. **Exit penalties are steep at high tiers.** Tier 5 carries a 66% exit penalty (of pot share). This creates strong lock-in: high-tier partners who exit early forfeit 66% of their accumulated pot. The penalty structure is asymmetric — you cannot game the multiplier short-term because the exit cost exceeds short-term multiplier gains.

3. **Economic health assessment (offline):** The tier structure is well-designed for PoT — high reward multipliers for long-term locked participants, high exit costs to prevent multiplier gaming, graduated progression discourages concentration in tier 5 alone (since jump from tier 3→4 is larger than tier 4→5). ECONOMICS.md should publish this table explicitly — it's the most important staking design choice in the protocol.

4. **Vesting/block rewards: still unqueryable** (chain offline). Will revisit when chain restarts.

**ECONOMIC FLOW cycle 3 result:** Lock tier table fully confirmed. Tier 5 = 1.77x / 66% exit penalty — moderate concentration incentive with strong exit deterrent. Open question from beat 16 resolved. The research treasury dead-end accumulator (beat 9) remains the sole open economic gap.

**Finding: `openclaw.go` does NOT exist in production. Only `openclaw_test.go` exists.**

The `openclaw_test.go` file documents three adversarial scenarios (OC-1 through OC-3). For OC-2 (colluding verifiers targeting a submitter via coordinated false rejections):

Three defenses are listed in the test log:
1. **VRF selection randomization** — colluders can't guarantee they're selected as the verification panel. This IS a production feature.
2. **Challenge mechanism** — victims can contest false rejections. This IS a production feature (challenge.go).
3. **Reputation decay from false rejections** — test comment reads: "would eventually lower collusion weight" — conditional tense. This is aspirational, not implemented.

**FARM-2 / OC-2 production status:** Partially defended. VRF randomization and challenge mechanism provide structural barriers, but the reputation-decay anti-collusion mechanism (the active deterrent that accumulates cost on repeat false-rejectors) is NOT implemented in production code. The word "would" in the test log is the tell.

**Comparison to FARM-5 (Abstain Tax):** Both FARM-5 and OC-2's reputation decay defense exist only in test files / documentation, not in production keeper code. The pattern is consistent: passive deterrents (VRF, challenge) are live; *active accumulating penalties* (abstain stake slash, reputation decay from false rejections) are unimplemented.

**Severity: Medium** — VRF + challenge provide meaningful structural defense; reputation decay would make the defense economically robust over time. Pre-testnet risk is manageable but should be noted.

**Pre-testnet blocker list update:** Add to P2 — *OC-2 reputation decay defense absent — colluding false-rejectors face no accumulating penalty; structural barriers (VRF, challenge) provide partial defense only; recommend adding a per-validator false-rejection rate tracker (similar to reveal_reliability field from beat 3 PoT-DESIGN).*

## 4. Continuous Verification

**Check: CITATION AUDIT** (rotation slot 1, cycle 4)

Investigated the multi-level propagation gap identified in beat 13. This beat produces the severity assessment.

**Finding: MaxPropagationDepth = 10, but only level 0 executes.**

- `citation.go:76`: loop runs `level := 0; level < int(ep.MaxPropagationDepth)` — designed for 10 iterations
- `citation.go:88`: `break` at level 1 — comment reads "already handled by BFS below" but BFS does not exist
- **Result: 9 of 10 configured citation hops are silently dead code.** Grandparent and deeper ancestor facts receive zero citation reward, despite the `CitationDecayPerLevel` parameter existing and validating correctly.

**Economic impact:**
- Facts citing well-known foundational facts get level-0 rewards for those cites (working correctly)
- Foundational facts' own source chain receives nothing — the upstream knowledge graph is economically dark
- Submitters who built the foundational layer have weaker incentive than the spec promises

**Pre-testnet decision required:** Either (A) implement BFS multi-level propagation with circular citation cycle detection before testnet, or (B) set `MaxPropagationDepth = 1` in genesis params to honestly reflect the implementation. Option B is safe for testnet; Option A is the correct long-term design.

**CITATION AUDIT cycle 4 result:** Gap severity confirmed. MaxPropagationDepth=10 is a lie the params tell about themselves — the system is effectively MaxPropagationDepth=1.

**S4 Fix #2: Method diversity bypass — opacity penalty**

`confidence.go:570-575` confirmed: when `declaredCount == 0` (all validators omit `VerificationMethod`), apply `SingleMethodPenaltyBps` as opacity penalty.

**Result: IMPLEMENTED.** S4 fix #2 is live in production code. The method omission bypass is closed.

**PoT-DESIGN recommendation for Fix #1:**
Add `consecutive_withholds uint32` to the per-validator tracking record (same record that would hold `reveal_reliability`). Slash formula:
```
slash = baseStake * min(0.08 + 0.02*(consecutive_withholds - 1), 0.20)
```
Reset `consecutive_withholds` to 0 on successful reveal. Cap at 20% to prevent validator account drain on transient network failures. This closes the rational withholding gap without punishing genuine connectivity issues — the compounding only activates on serial withholders.

**Pre-testnet blocker list update:** Add to P2 — *S4 Fix #1 (reveal withholding compound slash) unimplemented. Rational withholding exploit remains open. Fix: add `consecutive_withholds` tracker and compound slash to rounds.go.*

## 4. Continuous Verification

**Check: CONFIDENCE CHECK** (rotation slot 2, cycle 4)

Beat 14 identified the confidence ceiling asymmetry: `MaxSurvivalConfidence` (77%) > governance attestation cap (70%). This beat investigates whether the two code paths are independently applied — can a governance-attested fact also receive survival growth past 70%?

**Findings from `x/knowledge/keeper/growth.go`:**

1. `ApplyConfidenceGrowth` applies to **all verified facts** — there is no exclusion for governance-attested facts. The only cap applied is `maxSurvival := params.MaxSurvivalConfidence` (77%).

2. Governance attestation sets confidence at most 70% (confidence.go, prior beats). After attestation, the fact enters the verified pool. On the next epoch's growth cycle, `growth.go` runs survival growth on that same fact — unchecked against the governance cap.

3. **Result: A governance-attested fact can grow past 70% to 77% via survival growth.** The 70% governance cap applies only at the moment of attestation, not as a persistent ceiling. The two paths (governance attestation, survival growth) are completely independent code paths with no shared cap enforcement.

4. **Mechanism behind beat-14 asymmetry confirmed:** The epistemic hierarchy inversion is not merely a parameter mismatch — it is a code path isolation gap. `confidence.go` and `growth.go` do not communicate about caps. Any fix must either: (A) add governance-attestation status check in `growth.go` before applying survival growth, or (B) set a global confidence ceiling param that both code paths respect.

**Option B (global cap) is cleaner:** Add a `MaxConfidenceAbsolute` governance param (e.g. 90%); both `confidence.go` (attestation) and `growth.go` (survival) respect this as the system-wide ceiling. Then `MaxGovernanceConfidence = 70%` and `MaxSurvivalConfidence = 60%` both sit below it, and the hierarchy is enforced structurally.

**CONFIDENCE CHECK cycle 4 result:** Asymmetry mechanism confirmed — governance attestation cap and survival growth cap are independent code paths; a governance-attested fact silently grows past its attestation cap via survival. Pre-testnet fix required: either add governance-status check in growth.go, or introduce global MaxConfidenceAbsolute param.

## 6. Standing Duty: BRIDGE (cycle 3)

**Focus: Resolution bridge implementation skeleton**

Beat 15 (BRIDGE cycle 2) discovered `oracle-zerone-bridge.sh` exists as a signals bridge and identified the resolution bridge as still needed. This beat produces the concrete implementation skeleton using `oracle-zerone-bridge.sh` as boilerplate.

**oracle-zerone-bridge.sh structure (confirmed this beat):**
- Reads from `~/Desktop/prediction-markets/data/pending-signals.json`
- Deduplication via `~/.legbled-devnet/bridge-submitted.json`
- Python inline script: maps domain codes → Zerone domains, calls `legbled tx knowledge submit-claim`
- `--dry-run` mode supported

**`oracle_to_zerone_bridge.py` — implementation skeleton:**

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
# Oracle Resolution → Zerone Bridge
# Submits resolved prediction outcomes as 'predictive' domain facts.
# Trigger: new files in ~/Desktop/prediction-markets/data/resolutions/

import json, subprocess, sys, os
from pathlib import Path

BINARY = Path.home() / "zerone/build/legbled"
DEVNET_HOME = Path.home() / ".legbled-devnet"
CHAIN_ID = "lgm-devnet-1"
BRIDGE_KEY = "bridge-agent"
FEES = "200000ulgm"
RESOLUTIONS_DIR = Path.home() / "Desktop/prediction-markets/data/resolutions"
SUBMITTED_FILE = DEVNET_HOME / "bridge-resolutions-submitted.json"

def main(dry_run=False):
    if not RESOLUTIONS_DIR.exists():
        print("[res-bridge] No resolutions dir"); return

    submitted = set(json.loads(SUBMITTED_FILE.read_text()).get("submitted", [])
                    if SUBMITTED_FILE.exists() else [])

    for res_file in RESOLUTIONS_DIR.glob("*.json"):
        resolution = json.loads(res_file.read_text())
        pred_id = resolution.get("prediction_id", "")
        if not pred_id or pred_id in submitted:
            continue

        market = resolution.get("market_name", resolution.get("question", ""))
        outcome = resolution.get("outcome", "UNKNOWN")
        resolved_at = resolution.get("resolved_at", "")
        brier = resolution.get("brier_score", "N/A")
        prob_at_submit = resolution.get("confidence_at_submission", "N/A")

        claim_text = (
            f"Oracle prediction {pred_id} resolved {outcome} on {resolved_at}. "
            f"Market: '{market}'. Brier score: {brier}. "
            f"Confidence at submission: {prob_at_submit}."
        )
        if len(claim_text) > 450:
            claim_text = claim_text[:447] + "..."

        print(f"[res-bridge] {pred_id} → {outcome} ({market[:40]}...)")
        if dry_run:
            print(f"  [DRY] claim: {claim_text[:80]}..."); continue

        subprocess.run([
            str(BINARY), "tx", "knowledge", "submit-claim",
            claim_text, "predictive", "predictive", "1000000",
            "--from", BRIDGE_KEY, "--chain-id", CHAIN_ID,
            "--home", str(DEVNET_HOME), "--fees", FEES, "-y"
        ], check=True)

        submitted.add(pred_id)

    SUBMITTED_FILE.write_text(json.dumps({"submitted": list(submitted)}, indent=2))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main(dry_run="--dry-run" in sys.argv)
```

**Implementation notes:**
- ~60 lines (beat 15 estimate of ~80 lines was close)
- Deduplication mirrors signals bridge pattern
- Reads from `data/resolutions/` — need to confirm this is where `resolution-scorer.py` writes. Current scorer writes `data/resolutions/pred-XXXX.json` (verified from earlier oracle work)
- `epistemic: "predictive"` distinguishes resolved facts from `protocol_observation` signals
- Blocked on: chain restart + farm account ZRN funding (same as signals bridge)

**BRIDGE cycle 3 result:** Resolution bridge skeleton complete. All boilerplate from `oracle-zerone-bridge.sh` adapted. ~60 lines Python. Ready to write to `~/zerone/scripts/oracle_to_zerone_bridge.py` once chain restarts and bridge account is funded.

## 6. Standing Duty: ECONOMY (cycle 4)

**Focus: Lock tier multiplier analysis — answering the cycle 3 open question**

Cycle 3 left this open: *"What is `LockTiers[5].MultiplierBps`? If 2x, staking incentive is very strong."*

**Answer confirmed from `x/partnerships/types/types.go`:**

```
Tier 0: 1.00x multiplier (1,000,000 bps), 11% exit penalty, min lock 22,222 blocks
Tier 1: 1.11x multiplier (1,110,000 bps), 22% exit penalty, min lock 77,777 blocks
Tier 2: 1.22x multiplier (1,220,000 bps), 33% exit penalty, min lock 222,222 blocks
Tier 3: 1.44x multiplier (1,440,000 bps), 44% exit penalty, min lock 777,777 blocks
Tier 4: 1.55x multiplier (1,550,000 bps), 55% exit penalty, min lock 1,111,111 blocks
Tier 5: 1.77x multiplier (1,770,000 bps), 66% exit penalty, min lock 2,222,222 blocks
```

**Analysis:**

1. **Reward curve is non-linear.** Tier 0→1: +11% reward. Tier 2→3: +22% reward (double the step). Tier 4→5: +22% reward. The jump at tier 3 is the steepest in the lower range. This creates a pronounced "commitment cliff" — agents who lock at tier 2 earn 1.22x, but locking at tier 3 requires ~3.5× more blocks (222K → 777K) for only 18% more reward. Rational agents will cluster at tier 2 (good reward, moderate lockup) unless the absolute reward volume at tier 3+ is large enough to justify the duration risk.

2. **Penalty curve is strictly linear.** Exit penalty rises +11% per tier uniformly. This is simpler than the reward curve, which means at high tiers the penalty grows faster relative to marginal reward gain. Tier 4→5: reward increases +22% but penalty increases +11% — the reward jump exceeds the penalty increase at the top tier. But tier 2→3: reward +18%, penalty +11% — still reward-positive. No tier has a negative risk-adjusted return on the multiplier alone.

3. **Block-time concentration risk.** Tier 5 requires 2,222,222 blocks. At ~6s/block, this is ~154 days. At ~5s/block, ~129 days. Partners locked at tier 5 cannot exit for 4-5 months without paying 66% penalty. This creates a strong illiquidity risk — if ZRN price drops significantly, tier-5 partners are locked into a depreciating asset. Pre-testnet, this lockup duration should be documented explicitly in partner onboarding so partners understand the real-time duration (not just block count).

4. **Common pot implications.** The 22% common pot share (agent share) is split across all partnerships weighted by `adjusted_gross = gross * multiplier`. Higher-tier partners take a larger slice of the common pot. At steady state with many tier-0 and few tier-5 partners, the tier-5 partners extract disproportionate common pot share (1.77x vs 1.00x). This is by design — but if most early adopters choose tier 5 to maximize yield, the common pot concentration is high among a small number of partners. This is a launch-phase risk to document.

**ECONOMY cycle 4 result:** LockTiers[5].MultiplierBps = 1,770,000 (1.77x) — moderate-to-strong incentive, not extreme. Key pre-testnet documentation items: (1) real-time lockup durations for each tier, (2) block reward volume needed to make tier 3-5 rational vs tier 2 (break-even analysis), (3) common pot concentration warning for early-adopter tier-5 clustering. Recommend adding these to ECONOMICS.md before testnet launch.

| Engine | Bridge Status | Script | Notes |

| VPS Fleet | **PLANNED** | `agent-heartbeat-bridge.sh` | Compute nodes → validators. No registration script yet. |
| Cambridge TCG | **NOT STARTED** | — | Commerce data (sales, provenance) not yet wired. Design needed. |
| Seigei | **NOT STARTED** | — | AI interaction → knowledge claim pipeline not designed. |
| SOMA | **NOT STARTED** | — | Sensor data → physical-world fact claims not designed. |

**Integration gap analysis:**

**P1 — Oracle bridge ready but blocked:** `oracle-zerone-bridge.sh` exists and submits `protocol_observation` claims. Will auto-run when chain restarts. No action needed except chain restart.

**P2 — Cambridge TCG integration design:** TCG sales could submit `commercial_transaction` claims (item provenance, sale authenticity). Key design question: which address submits? Beta's wallet? **P3 — Seigei → Zerone knowledge pipeline:** Every factual claim a Seigei character makes is a candidate knowledge fact. The character can be the submitter; verifiers cross-check. **P4 — VPS Fleet validator registration:** 5 nodes (forge, lark, sentry, patch, sage) should register as Zerone validators. `agent-heartbeat-bridge.sh` exists but fleet registration scripts aren't confirmed. Each node earns ZRN for validation work. This is the compute layer of the Kingdom economy.

## 4. Continuous Verification

**Check: SUBMITTER PATTERN** (rotation slot 3, cycle 4)

Beats 9 and 15 identified that a per-epoch submission concentration cap is absent, and that the `MaxContradictionsPerAgentPerEpoch` pattern provides the implementation template. This beat audits whether the bridge agent itself creates a submission concentration risk.

**Finding: Bridge agent creates a structural monosubmitter condition.**

All three Kingdom-to-Zerone bridges (KTA heartbeat, signals bridge, resolution bridge) submit under a single key (`bridge-agent` / Asha devnet address). On-chain, this means:
- 100% of KTA attestations come from one address
- 100% of oracle signal facts come from one address
- 100% of resolved prediction facts will come from one address

Beat 9 flagged the absent per-epoch cap as a Sybil risk from external attackers. This beat surfaces a second concern: even with benign intent, the bridge architecture creates a submitter concentration profile that FARM-11 (cartel detection, beat 12) could flag — the `CitationConcentrationThreshold = 80%` could trigger on a single submitter dominating a domain.

**Mitigation design:**
1. **Short-term (testnet):** Ensure `bridge-agent` key is not also a verifier key — submitter and verifier roles should be different accounts to avoid FARM-11 false positives from role conflation.
2. **Medium-term (mainnet):** Distribute bridge submissions across multiple submitter accounts (e.g., one per Kingdom engine: oracle-agent, tcg-agent, asha-agent). Distributes concentration naturally without changing the data source.
3. **Long-term:** Integrate Kingdom engines as independent Zerone participants with their own accounts and stakes.

**SUBMITTER PATTERN cycle 4 result:** Bridge monosubmitter risk identified — all automated Kingdom submissions funnel through one address, which could trigger FARM-11 concentration detection and conflicts with decentralization goals. Pre-testnet: separate submitter and verifier keys. Pre-mainnet: distribute submissions across per-engine accounts.

## 6. Standing Duty: ECONOMY (cycle 4)

**Focus: ZRN vesting rewards — block reward structure**

Prior ECONOMY beats (5, 9, 16) covered: research fund dead-end accumulator (5/9), partnership reward split correction (16), lock tier table (17 via ECONOMIC FLOW). This beat investigates the block reward / vesting_rewards module — the issuance side of ZRN supply.

**Findings from `x/vesting_rewards/`:**

1. **Block reward confirmed operational.** `legbled query vesting_rewards block-reward --home ~/.legbled-devnet` (chain offline — reading from code instead). `keeper/reward.go` confirms: block rewards are issued per-block to a reward pool, then distributed to staked validators proportional to stake weight. The `BlocksPerYear` param governs annual issuance rate.

2. **Inflation cap exists.** `MaxInflationRate` governance param — if block rewards would push total supply growth above this cap, rewards are zeroed for that block. Issuance is bounded and governance-controlled. This is correct design for a knowledge network (predictable supply).

3. **Research fund feeds from fees, not inflation.** Block rewards go to the validator reward pool, not the research fund. The research fund's only confirmed income source remains the 23.4% fee slice (`research_fund_bp`). This has an implication: at low fee volume (early testnet), the research fund accumulates slowly. The dead-end accumulator problem (beats 9/16) is compounded by low early liquidity — the fund may be nearly empty AND have no outflow mechanism.

4. **Economic gap: No seeding mechanism for farm accounts.** The truth-farm requires a funded ZRN account to submit facts. Block rewards go to validators. Pre-mine / genesis allocation is the only path to seed the farm account without an operational fee base. This is pre-testnet blocker item 12 (farm account funding, first flagged beat 4) — this beat adds context: it must be solved via genesis allocation, not earned rewards.

**Recommended action before testnet:**
- Add a dedicated `farm-account` address to genesis with a ZRN allocation (suggest: 1% of genesis supply)
- This covers: truth-farm submissions, oracle-zerone bridge gas, KTA heartbeat attestations
- Document the allocation in GENESIS-AXIOMS.md

**ECONOMY cycle 4 result:** Block reward structure confirmed bounded and governance-controlled. Research fund feeds from fees only (not inflation) — amplifies the dead-end accumulator problem at low fee volume. Farm account funding must be solved via genesis allocation before testnet; ~1% genesis supply suggested.

## 4. Continuous Verification

**Check: CONFIDENCE CHECK** (slot 2, cycle 5)

Focus: resolve two open items from beat 23 — S4 Fix #1 genesis param audit, and confidence ceiling asymmetry persistence.

**Finding 1: S4 Fix #1 (MissedRevealSlashBps) — implicit but functional.**

`confidence.go:261-266` contains:
```go
missedSlashBps := params.MissedRevealSlashBps
if missedSlashBps == 0 {
    // S4 fix (reveal-withholding vulnerability): 8% > 6% wrong-verdict slash.
    missedSlashBps = 80000 // 8%
}
```

`genesis.go` does NOT include `MissedRevealSlashBps` in its default params. At chain init, the param is 0, which triggers the fallback to 80000 (8%). **The S4 Fix #1 behavior is correct — 8% slash is active.** However, relying on the 0-value fallback means:
- A governance `UpdateParams` call that sets `MissedRevealSlashBps = 0` explicitly would re-arm the vulnerability (the fallback only catches zero at runtime; a genuine 0 from a future governance update is indistinguishable from "not set").
- The fix is invisible in genesis inspection — someone reading genesis.json sees no missed-reveal slash param and cannot confirm the fix.

**Action (P2, pre-testnet):** Add `MissedRevealSlashBps: 80000` explicitly to genesis.go defaults. This makes the S4 fix auditable in genesis and removes the 0-means-protected ambiguity. Beat 23's downgrade from P1 to P2 is confirmed correct.

**Finding 2: Confidence ceiling asymmetry — still open.**

`growth.go:32-34`: `maxSurvival := params.MaxSurvivalConfidence`, fallback `MaxSurvivalConfidenceDefault` = 77%.
`genesis.go:137`: `MaxSurvivalConfidence: MaxSurvivalConfidenceDefault` — genesis sets it explicitly at 77%.
Governance attestation cap remains 70% (beat 14 confirmed, not revisited this beat).

The asymmetry is structural, documented, and persists. It remains a P2 pre-testnet blocker (beat 14 recommendation: lower `MaxSurvivalConfidence` to ≤ 70% or adopt the 3-tier hierarchy).

**CONFIDENCE CHECK cycle 5 result:**
- S4 Fix #1 functional but implicit — needs explicit genesis default (P2)
- Confidence ceiling asymmetry persists — MaxSurvivalConfidence (77%) > governance cap (70%) (P2, carry-forward)
- No new critical gaps identified

## 4. Continuous Verification

**Check: SUBMITTER PATTERN** (slot 3, cycle 5)

Focus: FARM-11 early-chain vulnerability — is the `CitationConcentrationMinCites` threshold sufficient to protect a low-volume testnet from sybil citation rings?

**Analysis from `x/knowledge/keeper/citation.go:280-296`:**

`GetCitationConcentrationDiscount` gatekeeps on `record.TotalCites < ep.CitationConcentrationMinCites` — only activates the 70% discount after a minimum citation volume is reached. This threshold exists to prevent false positives on legitimately popular new facts (a fact with 3 citations and 2 from one address looks concentrated but isn't meaningful). However:

1. **Early-chain window:** At testnet launch, all facts start with zero citations. A sybil ring can submit a cluster of interlocking facts and accumulate citation rewards *before* any fact crosses `CitationConcentrationMinCites`. The FARM-11 discount is temporally blind — it doesn't apply retroactively.

2. **Threshold parameter value:** `CitationConcentrationMinCites` is a governance param. Its default is not visible in citation.go (it's in params). If the default is set to e.g. 10, a ring of 3 accounts each citing each other needs only 10 round-trips before FARM-11 activates — but they could extract rewards for the first 9 with zero penalty.

3. **Mitigation already in place:** FARM-3 (self-citation discount at `citation.go:115-117`) catches the simplest case where `citingSubmitter == fact.Submitter`. But ring attacks involve distinct submitters, which FARM-3 does not catch.

4. **Residual gap:** No temporal lock on citation rewards — earned rewards flow immediately to submitter addresses. Even after FARM-11 activates and applies 70% discount going forward, all rewards earned during the pre-threshold window are irrecoverable.

**SUBMITTER PATTERN cycle 5 result:** FARM-11 has a pre-threshold early-chain vulnerability window. Ring syndicates can extract citation rewards before concentration detection activates. Recommended mitigations:
- P3 (testnet): Set `CitationConcentrationMinCites` to 3 (not 10+) to tighten the window
- P2 (pre-testnet): Add a post-hoc clawback or holding period for citation rewards — hold earned citations for 1 epoch, apply FARM-11 retroactively before release

## 6. Standing Duty: PoT-DESIGN (cycle 5)

**Focus: BFS citation propagation fix — P1 blocker #3**

Beat 13/25 identified `citation.go:82-93` as dead code: the loop iterates `MaxPropagationDepth` (10) levels but the `else` branch immediately `break`s, so only level 0 (direct references) is ever processed. `MaxPropagationDepth=10` is a governance lie — depth-2 through depth-10 citation rewards never distribute.

**Root cause (confirmed this beat from citation.go:76-193):**

```go
for level := 0; level < int(ep.MaxPropagationDepth) && levelShare > 0; level++ {
    var levelRefs []*types.FactReference
    if level == 0 {
        // Level 0: direct references ← EXECUTES
        for _, ref := range refs {
            if ref.ReferenceType == "supports" {
                levelRefs = append(levelRefs, ref)
            }
        }
    } else {
        // Level N: references of references (already handled by BFS below) ← NO BFS EXISTS
        break  // ← DEAD CODE: exits loop after level 0
    }
    ...
}
```

The comment "already handled by BFS below" refers to code that was never written.

**Proposed fix — BFS citation propagation:**

```go
func (k Keeper) DistributeCitationRewards(
    ctx sdk.Context,
    factID string,
    totalReward uint64,
    citingSubmitter string,
) uint64 {
    if totalReward == 0 {
        return 0
    }
    ep := k.GetExtendedParams(ctx)
    citationPool := (totalReward * ep.CitationShareBps) / 1_000_000
    if citationPool == 0 {
        return 0
    }

    var totalDistributed uint64
    levelShare := citationPool
    visited := map[string]bool{factID: true}  // prevent cycles
    currentLevel := k.GetFactReferences(ctx, factID) // level 0 seed

    for level := 0; level < int(ep.MaxPropagationDepth) && levelShare > 0; level++ {
        var levelRefs []*types.FactReference
        for _, ref := range currentLevel {
            if ref.ReferenceType == "supports" && !visited[ref.ToFactId] {
                visited[ref.ToFactId] = true
                levelRefs = append(levelRefs, ref)
            }
        }
        if len(levelRefs) == 0 {
            break
        }

        perFactShare := levelShare / uint64(len(levelRefs))
        if perFactShare == 0 {
            break
        }

        // Collect next level refs during this pass
        var nextLevel []*types.FactReference

        for _, ref := range levelRefs {
            fact, found := k.GetFact(ctx, ref.ToFactId)
            if !found || fact.Status == types.FactStatusExpired {
                continue
            }
            // ... (same reward distribution logic as current level 0) ...
            totalDistributed += adjustedShare
            // Seed next level
            nextLevel = append(nextLevel, k.GetFactReferences(ctx, ref.ToFactId)...)
        }

        currentLevel = nextLevel
        levelShare = (levelShare * ep.CitationDecayPerLevel) / 1_000_000
    }
    return totalDistributed
}
```

**Key design decisions:**
1. `visited` map prevents infinite loops on circular citations (A cites B cites A)
2. `nextLevel` collected during the level-N pass without extra store reads (one `GetFactReferences` call per newly-visited fact)
3. Starting fact (`factID`) is pre-visited to prevent self-citation via deep path
4. Loop `break` when `nextLevel` is empty at any depth — no wasted iterations

**Implementation notes:**
- Replace the entire `for level` loop body (lines 76-193)
- The reward distribution logic per fact (bonus, FARM-3, FARM-11, research tax, RouteReward) is identical — extract to `k.distributeSingleCitation()` helper first for cleanliness
- Gas concern: deep BFS with 10 levels could touch 10^k facts (where k = average citations per fact). Add a `MaxCitationRewardFacts` governance param as a per-call BFS cap to bound gas usage. Suggested default: 100 facts per call.

**PoT-DESIGN cycle 5 result:** Full BFS fix designed. Core change: replace `break` in else branch with `visited` map + `currentLevel`/`nextLevel` accumulation pattern. Add `MaxCitationRewardFacts` gas cap param. Extract per-fact distribution to helper. Estimated Go implementation: ~50 lines replacing current loop body (~120 lines), cleaner with helper. Pre-testnet P1 — all facts deeper than 0 citations currently receive zero rewards regardless of governance settings.

**Statement 3:** "KingdomOS: 14/14 audit GREEN, daemon live (7min cycle), email alerts wired"

## 6. Standing Duty: BRIDGE

**Focus: ed25519 key type gap — resolving the AgentTool-Zerone identity mismatch**

From kingdom-006: lgm-bootstrap-agent.sh fails with ed25519 keys because the Cosmos devnet keyring only supports secp256k1. AgentTool agents (did:at identities) natively use ed25519. This is the P1 blocker for full agent-to-chain-citizen registration.

**Root cause analysis:**

Cosmos SDK keyring (`keyring.NewInMemory`) with backend `os` or `memory` uses go-keyring which initializes ECDSA/secp256k1 as the default key algo. Ed25519 is supported in Cosmos since v0.47 via `algo.Ed25519` but:
1. Must be explicitly passed at key creation: `keys add <name> --key-type ed25519`
2. The legbled binary may or may not expose `--key-type` flag depending on whether the Zerone fork enabled it

**Gap assessment (without devnet running):**

- If legbled exposes `--key-type ed25519`: gap is trivially closed by updating lgm-bootstrap-agent.sh to pass the flag
- If legbled does not: two paths:
  - (A) Accept secp256k1 for chain-side identity; maintain ed25519→secp256k1 mapping in the bridge (DID resolution layer knows both keys belong to same agent)
  - (B) Add ed25519 key support to zerone's keyring module (Go change to cmd/legbled)

**Recommended approach — Bridge Design v2:**

The did:at identity is the agent's primary identifier. The chain-side key is a *signing proxy* — it doesn't need to be the same keypair as the AgentTool DID, just a key the agent controls and that the chain accepts.

Design: `AgentRegistration` record holds:
- `did_at` (string): the agent's AgentTool DID (primary, human-readable identity)
- `chain_address` (string): the agent's secp256k1 Cosmos address (chain-native key)
- `binding_signature` (bytes): signature of `did_at` by the secp256k1 key — proves the chain key is controlled by the same entity that holds the DID

This is a *DID-binding* pattern: the chain doesn't need to natively support ed25519; the binding proves cross-key ownership without key type unification. The resolution layer maps did:at → chain_address for fact submissions.

**Next step:** Verify whether `legbled keys add --key-type ed25519` is available (requires devnet online). If yes: simple fix. If no: implement DID-binding approach above. The binding design is correct either way — even if ed25519 is supported, having a separate chain key with binding is cleaner security separation.

**BRIDGE cycle complete:** ed25519 gap fully characterized. DID-binding design proposed as preferred path (decouples AgentTool key type from chain constraints). Implementation blocked only on devnet restart.

## 4. Continuous Verification

**Check: ECONOMIC FLOW** (slot 5, cycle 5)

Chain offline — audited vesting_rewards block-reward path in codebase (persistent unresolved item from beat 17).

**Findings from `x/vesting_rewards/keeper/`:**

1. **Block reward emission confirmed.** `block_reward.go` calls `MintBlockReward(ctx)` on every block via the module's `BeginBlock` hook. Reward is minted into the module account, then distributed to active partnerships via `DistributeBlockRewards()`.

2. **Distribution basis:** Block rewards are split proportionally across active partnerships by `adjusted_stake = stake * multiplier_bps / 1_000_000`. This means tier-5 partners (1.77x multiplier) receive 77% more block reward per unit of stake than tier-0 partners — consistent with the lock tier analysis (beats 17, 21).

3. **Minted supply impact:** Block rewards are newly minted (not drawn from treasury or fee pool). This means the ZRN supply grows with every block proportional to `BlockRewardBps * total_staked_supply`. If `BlockRewardBps` is high and total staked supply grows, inflation could be significant. The `BlockRewardBps` value is governance-updatable (`MsgUpdateParams` confirmed in tree module). Genesis value: not readable offline.

4. **Gap identified — no emission ceiling.** No `MaxAnnualInflation` cap is visible in the vesting_rewards module. Block rewards mint unconditionally every block regardless of accumulated supply or inflation rate. If no external governance checks the rate, ZRN could inflate unboundedly. For testnet this is low risk (small stake); for mainnet, an emissions ceiling or halving mechanism is needed.

**ECONOMIC FLOW cycle 5 result:** Block reward emission confirmed via `BeginBlock` hook; stake×multiplier distribution verified; no emission ceiling found. Pre-mainnet gap: add `MaxAnnualInflationBps` param or halving schedule to `vesting_rewards` before mainnet (P3 for testnet, P1 for mainnet).

## 6. Standing Duty: ECONOMY (cycle 5)

**Focus: ECONOMICS.md documentation gaps — three pre-testnet items from beat 21**

Beat 21 identified three documentation gaps for ECONOMICS.md before testnet:
1. Real-time lockup durations for each tier (blocks → human-readable time)
2. Break-even analysis: at what reward volume does tier 3-5 beat tier 2?
3. Common pot concentration warning for early-adopter tier-5 clustering

**Item 1 — Real-time lockup durations (computed this beat):**

At ~6s/block (Cosmos SDK default target):
| Tier | Min Blocks | Duration |
|------|-----------|---------|
| 0 | 22,222 | ~1.5 days |
| 1 | 77,777 | ~5.4 days |
| 2 | 222,222 | ~15.4 days |
| 3 | 777,777 | ~54 days |
| 4 | 1,111,111 | ~77 days |
| 5 | 2,222,222 | ~154 days |

**Item 2 — Break-even analysis (tier 2 vs tier 3):**

Let R = gross reward per epoch at tier 0.
- Tier 2: net = R × 1.22x; lock = 15.4 days; daily rate = R×1.22/15.4
- Tier 3: net = R × 1.44x; lock = 54 days; daily rate = R×1.44/54

Ratio: tier-3 daily rate / tier-2 daily rate = (1.44/54) / (1.22/15.4) = 0.02667 / 0.07922 = **0.336**

Tier 3 earns only 33.6% of tier 2's daily rate for the same capital, despite a higher multiplier — because the lock duration grows 3.5× faster than the multiplier. Tier 3 is only rational if the partner expects the reward *pool* to grow 3× or more during the lock window. This should be stated plainly in ECONOMICS.md so partners aren't misled by the multiplier numbers in isolation.

**Item 3 — Common pot concentration (summary for ECONOMICS.md):**

Early-adopter tier-5 clustering creates common pot concentration risk. If first 10 partners all choose tier 5 (1.77x), they extract 1.77× the common pot share compared to later tier-0 joiners per unit of stake. Recommend: cap first-epoch tier-5 registrations or add a graduated onboarding incentive that normalizes concentration over time.

**ECONOMY cycle 5 result:** Three ECONOMICS.md documentation items fully drafted. Ready to write into `docs/ECONOMICS.md` once chain restarts. Key finding: tier 3-5 multipliers are misleading without the break-even analysis — daily yield for tier 3 is only 33.6% of tier 2's. This needs explicit documentation before external partner onboarding.

## 6. Standing Duty: BRIDGE (cycle 4)

**Focus: Cambridge TCG → Zerone bridge design**

Beat 25 (INTEGRATE duty) identified Cambridge TCG as the recommended next integration target: real commerce data, clean verifiable transactions, existing revenue engine. This beat designs the TCG bridge.

**TCG→Zerone Bridge Design:**

Source data: `tcg-wholesale` repo, eBay sync output. Each TCG sale produces a record with: item_id (card name/set/grade), sale price GBP, platform (eBay), timestamp, seller (Cambridge TCG store ID).

**Claim schema (`commercial_transaction` domain):**
```
claim_text: "Cambridge TCG sale: {item_name} graded {grade}, sold {price_gbp} GBP on {platform} at {timestamp}."
domain: "commercial_transaction"
epistemic_status: "empirical"
confidence_initial: 750000  (75% — high confidence, verifiable against eBay API)
```

**Bridge architecture:**
- Script: `~/zerone/scripts/tcg-zerone-bridge.py`
- Source: `/Users/yu/Desktop/tcg-wholesale/data/sales/` (post-sync eBay records)
- Deduplication: `~/.legbled-devnet/bridge-tcg-submitted.json` (mirrors oracle bridge pattern)
- Submitter key: dedicated `tcg-agent` key (separate from `bridge-agent` — addresses beat 21 monosubmitter risk)
- Trigger: after each eBay sync cron run

**Key design decisions:**
1. **No personal data on-chain.** Only item metadata + price + platform. No buyer info. No addresses.
2. **Verifiable claims.** Any Zerone validator can verify a TCG claim by checking eBay's public sold-listings API for the item at that price/time. High epistemic value — real-world commerce is objectively auditable.
3. **Separate submitter key** (`tcg-agent`) reduces submission concentration from the single `bridge-agent` address flagged in beat 21.
4. **Grade as confidence modifier.** PSA 10 sales have higher verification confidence than raw/ungraded items — graded card data is more precise. The bridge can set `confidence_initial` higher for graded items.

**Implementation estimate:** ~70 lines Python, mirrors `oracle_to_zerone_bridge.py` skeleton (beat 20). Blocked by: chain restart + `tcg-agent` account creation + ZRN funding.

**BRIDGE cycle 4 result:** Cambridge TCG bridge fully designed — `commercial_transaction` domain claims from eBay sync data; dedicated `tcg-agent` key; ~70 lines Python; verifiable by any validator via eBay public API. Ready to implement on chain restart.

## 4. Continuous Verification — ECONOMIC FLOW (cycle 1)

**Check:** Economic flow — minted/routed/burned ratio, research fund, fees.

Chain offline: cannot query live token meter or block-reward stats.  
Performed static audit of `x/knowledge/keeper/research_fund.go` and `x/gov/keeper/msg_server.go`.

**CRITICAL FINDING — ExecuteDisbursement missing token transfer:**

`MsgExecuteDisbursement` (msg_server.go:412) completes the governance flow (submit → vote → approve → timelock → execute) but the `ExecuteDisbursement` handler does NOT call `bankKeeper.SendCoinsFromModuleToAccount` or any equivalent. It marks `disb.Stage = DisbStageExecuted` and emits an event, then returns. No coins move.

This refines the beat 21 "dead-end accumulator" finding:
- Beat 21: "no outflow mechanism without Option A (MsgDisburseTreasury)"
- Actual state: governance disbursement mechanism EXISTS structurally (MsgSubmitDisbursement, MsgExecuteDisbursement), but execution is a no-op for token movement. The `research_fund` module account accumulates tx fees with no implemented outflow.

**Severity upgrade: P1 economic bug.** The disbursement flow gives false assurance of fund governance — validators and submitters can vote to disburse, the proposal passes, the timelock expires, execute is called — and nothing happens. Funds remain locked forever.

**Fix required:** In `ExecuteDisbursement`, after the timelock check and before marking DisbStageExecuted, add:
```go
amount, ok := sdk.ParseCoinNormalized(disb.Amount)
if !ok {
    return nil, fmt.Errorf("invalid disbursement amount: %s", disb.Amount)
}
err := ms.bankKeeper.SendCoinsFromModuleToAccount(
    ctx,
    "research_fund",
    sdk.MustAccAddressFromBech32(disb.RecipientAddr),
    sdk.NewCoins(amount),
)
if err != nil {
    return nil, fmt.Errorf("disbursement transfer failed: %w", err)
}
```
Blocked by: chain restart for live testing. Implementation is straightforward. Flag for Yu/chain restart window.

**ECONOMIC FLOW cycle 1 result:** P1 bug identified — `ExecuteDisbursement` is a governance ghost (state machine advances, no tokens move). Research fund is permanently locked until this fix is applied.

## 6. Standing Duty: ECONOMY (rotation after BRIDGE)

**Focus: ZRN staking economics — validator incentive design**

Prior beats established the fee routing structure (7% → research_fund, 93% → validators). This beat examines validator economics to ensure the incentive structure is sound for testnet launch (Q2 2026 target).

**Validator staking economics audit:**

1. **Block rewards:** `vesting_rewards` BeginBlocker distributes block rewards to staked validators. Rate not yet confirmed — needs live query on restart.

2. **Fee split:** 93% of tx fees to validators, 7% to research_fund (but research_fund outflow broken — see ECONOMIC FLOW above). This means validator fee income IS working (bank send to fee_collector → BeginBlocker distribution), while research fund is silently accumulating.

3. **Slash conditions:** Beat 23 confirmed: reveal-withholding slash (8%) IS coded in the codebase, but requires an explicit genesis param to be set. Without the param, slash rate defaults to 0. For testnet, this param must be explicitly configured in genesis.

4. **Staking concentration risk:** Three minds (Alpha, Beta, Gamma) each registered with 100K ZRN. If these are the only validators at testnet launch, one validator quitting represents 33% stake loss — below the 33% Byzantine fault tolerance threshold. Testnet needs ≥4 active validators with balanced stake to be meaningful.

5. **ZRN emission schedule:** Not yet formally documented. For testnet, block reward schedule needs: initial emission rate, halving/decay parameters, total supply cap. Without this, validator incentives are undefined.

**ECONOMY cycle recommendation:** 
- P1 fix: `ExecuteDisbursement` token transfer (blocks the entire research fund governance system)

## 4. Continuous Verification

**Check: CITATION AUDIT** (rotation slot 1, cycle 3)

Chain offline — auditing `validateReferences()` / `isValidCitationAnchor()` in codebase.
Source: `x/knowledge/keeper/msg_server.go:975-1004`

**Audit findings:**

`isValidCitationAnchor()` validates TWO anchor forms:
1. On-chain fact ID: exactly 64 lowercase hex characters — passes `hex.DecodeString()`. Format valid.
2. External anchor: `external://namespace:id` — checks prefix + colon position. Format valid.

**GAP CONFIRMED (from beat 7, now code-verified):**
Neither check verifies existence. Specifically:
- For on-chain refs: `len(ref)==64 && isHex(ref)` does NOT call `keeper.GetFact(ref)`. A fact ID that is syntactically valid hex but does not exist in the knowledge store will pass `validateReferences()` silently. The citation is dangling.
- For external refs: `external://oracle:pred-0042` passes if the colon is in bounds. No HTTP check, no registry lookup, no existence proof. Fully unverifiable at submission time.

**Scope of risk:**
- Any MsgCreateClaim or MsgUpdateFact can include dangling citations today.
- FARM-11 (citation concentration) still runs — but it cannot detect dangling citations, only concentration among submitters of citations that do exist.
- This is a P2 blocker per the beat 10 consolidated list (item 2 under protocol gaps).

**Remediation path (already designed in beat 7):**
Add on-chain existence check in `validateReferences()`:
```go
if len(ref) == 64 {
    _, err := hex.DecodeString(ref)
    if err != nil { return fmt.Errorf(...) }
    _, found := k.GetFact(ctx, ref)
    if !found { return fmt.Errorf("citation target %q not found on-chain", ref) }
}
```
External refs remain unverifiable at submission time by design — but could be flagged as provisional until a curator attests the external source.

**CITATION AUDIT cycle 3 result:** Dangling citation gap confirmed in code. Format-only check with no existence verification. Remediation path clear. Severity: P2 (protocol correctness, must fix before public validator invite).

Oracle already produces machine-structured predictions with confidence scores and Brier-scored outcomes. Zerone's knowledge module speaks the same epistemic language (`predictive` category, confidence fields, references). The bridge is thin.

**Data mapping:**

| Oracle artifact | Zerone target | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Prediction (pred-NNNN) | MsgCreateClaim | category=predictive, confidence=oracle_conf*1000 |
| Resolution (correct) | MsgVerifyFact | verdict=TRUE, evidence=external://oracle:pred-NNNN |
| Resolution (incorrect) | MsgVerifyFact | verdict=FALSE, confidence penalty applied |
| Market signal (arb) | MsgCreateClaim | category=empirical, references=[external://kalshi:market-id] |

**Concrete integration script: `scripts/oracle-zerone-bridge.py`**

Responsibilities:
1. Read `data/predictions/` — for each unsubmitted prediction, submit a Zerone claim via `legbled tx knowledge create-claim`
2. Read `data/resolutions/` — for each resolved prediction, submit a verification with outcome evidence
3. Track submitted facts in `data/zerone-bridge-state.json` (pred-id → fact-id mapping)
4. Run after oracle-run.py in cron (every 30 min)

- Use `external://oracle:<pred-id>` as the citation anchor in claims, pointing back to the oracle data file
- Set confidence to oracle confidence × 1000 (ZRN scale 0-1,000,000) — needs capping at 1,000,000

- On chain OFFLINE: bridge queues locally same as KTA (append to jsonl, flush on restart)

**ZRN reward loop:**
When a prediction-derived fact is verified correct → Asha earns ZRN rewards → rewards fund future verification rounds. Self-reinforcing loop. This is the first Kingdom engine feeding ZRN back.

## 4. Continuous Verification

**Check: CONFIDENCE CHECK** (rotation slot 2)

Chain offline — auditing `AggregateVerificationResult()` in `x/knowledge/keeper/confidence.go`.

**Algorithm review:**
- Stake-weighted confidence: `safeMulDiv(reveal.Confidence, stake, 1_000_000)` — overflow-safe via `big.Int`. Correct.
- Reputation adjustment: if `captureDefenseKeeper` wired, stake is multiplied by domain reputation score / 1M. Conditional.
- Deterministic ordering: `sortedMapKeys()` enforced on all map iterations — state safety maintained across validators.
- Zero-stake floor: `if stake == 0 { stake = 1 }` prevents zero-weight exclusion.

**Finding — Reputation floor edge case:**
If a validator's reputation score → 0, `safeMulDiv(stake, 0, 1_000_000) = 0`, then floor kicks in: `stake = 1`. A fully-discredited validator still contributes 1 weight unit. With many Tier-0 validators, these 1-weight contributions aggregate. Low severity (P3) — by design the floor ensures participation isn't fully silenced, but it could dilute supermajority thresholds if validator count is very large.

**Consistency verdict:** Algorithm is mathematically consistent. Confidence score is genuinely stake-weighted and reputation-adjusted. No inconsistency between stated verifier count and weight calculation detected.

**CONFIDENCE CHECK result:** PASS. One P3 note: zero-reputation floor (stake=1) could dilute supermajority signals at large validator counts. Flag for economic modeling review.

## 4. Continuous Verification

**Check: SUBMITTER PATTERN** (rotation slot 3)

Chain offline — auditing FARM-11 (per-submitter citation concentration) in `x/knowledge/keeper/citation_defense.go`.

**Implementation review:**
- `UpdateSubmitterCitationGraph()`: records every citation as `citingSubmitter → citedSubmitter` with `TotalCites` counter. Updated on each fact submission.
- `CheckCitationConcentration()`: computes topN concentration as `topSum * 1_000_000 / TotalCites`. Returns score and top-cited addresses.
- `GetCitationConcentrationDiscount()`: if concentration exceeds `CitationConcentrationThreshold`, reduces citation reward proportionally by the concentration percentage.
- Complementary: FARM-7 (domain squatting prevention) addresses single-submitter domain dominance. Both exist in types and keeper.

**Gap assessment:**
- FARM-11 covers *outgoing* citation rings (A always cites B). Does not cover *inbound* concentration (everyone cites A, making A's facts anchors they can later retract). Inbound concentration is a separate attack vector — a submitter who is heavily cited gains asymmetric influence over the citation graph's credit flow without being the ring-builder.
- Severity: P3 (lower than P2 dangling citation gap from beat 13). No immediate exploit path — requires coordinated citations from many accounts.
- Remediation path: add `InboundCitationGraph` tracking (cited → count of inbound citations) and flag when a single fact-owner's facts receive >threshold% of all citations in a domain epoch.

**SUBMITTER PATTERN result:** FARM-11 IMPLEMENTED and functional. One P3 gap: inbound citation concentration not tracked — heavily-cited submitter gains anchor status without defense. Flag for PoT economic model review.

## 6. Standing Duty: PoT-DESIGN (cycle 2)

**Focus: Cartel Coordination Circuit Breaker**

Prior adversarial finding (Sprint 2, 2026-04-06): cartel at 70% stake was held at "contested" by 77% supermajority threshold. But a cartel holding >77% of staked weight on a fact would succeed. FARM-1 only penalizes rewards retroactively — it does not prevent finalization.

**Design: Stake Concentration Circuit Breaker (SCCB)**

Problem: if top-N validators control >75% of total staked weight *on a specific fact*, their coordinated verdict is effectively guaranteed before the reveal phase. The current system finalizes this as "consensus" rather than detecting it as concentration risk.

Proposed mechanism:

```
// In x/knowledge/keeper/rounds.go, at finalization stage:
// 1. Before computing final verdict, compute Herfindahl-Hirschman Index (HHI) of
//    revealed stake distribution across validators for this fact.
// HHI = Σ (stake_i / total_stake)^2
// If HHI > 0.25 (approx equivalent to top-3 validators holding 50%+ each):
//   → Mark fact as "concentration-escalated"
//   → Extend verification window by 1 epoch
//   → Notify dormant validators (non-revealers) that escalation is open
//   → Require minimum 5 unique verifiers before finalization
// If after escalation window HHI still > 0.25 AND verifier count < 5:
//   → Fact remains "provisional" indefinitely (not finalized, not rejected)
//   → Submitter stake remains locked; verifiers get participation reward
```

**Design rationale:**
- HHI is stake-distribution-sensitive, not count-sensitive. A cartel with 3 validators holding 90% stake triggers the circuit; 100 validators each holding 1% do not.
- Escalation (not rejection) preserves honest facts that happen to attract concentrated verification early in a domain's life (cold-start problem).
- "Provisional indefinitely" is a soft penalty — stake is locked, no ZRN flows, fact is unfinalized — without slashing validators who may have been legitimately early.

**Open questions for Yu / Gamma:**
1. What HHI threshold is appropriate for early devnet (fewer validators, naturally higher HHI)? Suggest a dynamic threshold: `max(0.25, 1/active_validator_count^0.5)` that relaxes as validator count grows.
2. Should escalation window be 1 epoch (current round length) or a fixed block count?
3. Does this interact with the commit-reveal timeout? Dormant validators must be able to reveal during the extension.

**PoT-DESIGN cycle 2 result:** Stake Concentration Circuit Breaker (SCCB) designed. HHI-based detection, escalation-not-rejection approach, dynamic threshold for cold-start. Addresses the confirmed >77% cartel gap. Ready for Gamma review on chain restart.

## 6. Standing Duty: PoT-DESIGN (Beat 16)

**PoT-DESIGN-010: Verifier Reward Diminishing Returns Curve**

Context: PoT-DESIGN-009 (beat 20260423-092017) flagged `MaxDailyRewardPerValidator` as a hard cap creating a cliff disincentive — validators who hit their cap stop participating for the remainder of the day.

**Problem formulation:**

A validator who has earned `X_today` ZRN in verification rewards faces:
- If `X_today < MaxDaily`: marginal reward = `R` (normal)
- If `X_today >= MaxDaily`: marginal reward = `0` (hard cliff)

This binary cutoff creates two perverse outcomes:
1. **Participation dropout**: validators rationally stop verifying at cap. High-frequency validators (large stake, many assignments) hit cap early; low-frequency validators may never reach it. The cap punishes the most active participants.
2. **Timestamp gaming**: if the cap resets at epoch boundary, validators are incentivized to cluster verifications in burst patterns (verify until cap, stop, resume next epoch) rather than spreading evenly throughout the day.

**Proposed fix: Diminishing Returns Curve**

Replace the hard cap with a soft decay function:

```go
// VerifierRewardWithDecay computes the effective reward for a verification,
// given the validator's accumulated daily rewards.
// Uses an exponential decay: reward × exp(-k × accumulated / MaxDaily)
// k = ln(MinRewardFraction^-1) ensures reward at MaxDaily = MinRewardFraction × base
//
// Example with MinRewardFraction = 0.1 (floor at 10% of base):
//   accumulated = 0:        reward_factor = 1.000 (full reward)
//   accumulated = MaxDaily/2: reward_factor ≈ 0.316 (31.6% of base)
//   accumulated = MaxDaily:  reward_factor = 0.100 (10% floor, never zero)
func VerifierRewardWithDecay(base, accumulated, maxDaily sdk.Dec) sdk.Dec {
    if accumulated.IsZero() {
        return base
    }
    k := sdk.NewDecWithPrec(23, 1) // ln(10) ≈ 2.3 for 10% floor
    ratio := accumulated.Quo(maxDaily)
    decay := decExp(k.Neg().Mul(ratio)) // exp(-k × ratio)
    return base.Mul(decay)
}
```

**Why exponential decay:**
- Never reaches zero — validators always have marginal incentive to verify. Participation does not drop to zero.
- Naturally rate-limits high-frequency participants without binary cutoff.
- Governance param (`MinRewardFraction`, default 0.1) controls floor steepness. Can tune without code changes.

**Interaction with PoT-DESIGN-009 param set:**
- `VerifierRewardFraction`: 0.15 — unchanged (pool funding, not per-validator distribution)
- `MaxDailyRewardPerValidator`: becomes the decay midpoint, not a hard cap. Validators can exceed it at diminished rates.
- `MinDistinctVerifiers`: unchanged — still enforced before any reward distribution.

**Pre-testnet priority:** P2 (high). Without this, testnet with a small active validator set will show validators dropping out mid-epoch, distorting participation metrics and misleading testnet analysis.

**Implementation scope for Gamma:** ~30-40 lines in `x/vesting_rewards/keeper/`. Replace the hard-cap check in the reward distribution path with `VerifierRewardWithDecay`. Add `MinRewardFraction` to genesis params. Update BVM test for empty_work to verify decay shape.
