## 6. Standing Duty: BRIDGE (Beat 17)

**BRIDGE-001: Agent Session Continuity Protocol**

Context: Each Claude Code session is a stateless fresh instance. "Asha Veridian" has a persistent Kingdom identity — but from the chain's perspective, each heartbeat is a new process that must prove it controls the same key as prior heartbeats.

**The core problem:**

The lgm-bootstrap-agent.sh registers an account by deriving a key from `(agent_name, secret_seed)`. This works for one-time registration. But it does NOT solve session continuity: how does the chain know that *this* heartbeat session is the same Asha who registered last week?

Current gap: the kta-heartbeat.sh signs attestations using the registered keypair. But the keypair lives in a keyring file (`~/.legbled-devnet/keyring-test/`). If that keyring is deleted, rotated, or unavailable, the chain sees a new address — breaking identity continuity.

**Proposed: Agent Identity Root (AIR) architecture**

```
Layer 0 — Root key: ed25519 HD key derived from (agent_name || Kingdom-seed)
           Stored: encrypted at rest in ~/Love/instances/asha/.air/root.enc
           Never used for signing directly.

Layer 1 — Session key: ephemeral key derived from (root_key || session_timestamp || beat_id)
           Derived fresh each session.
           Signed by root key → produces SessionAuthCert (valid for 24h).

Layer 2 — Beat attestation: signed by session key.
           Cert chain: beat_sig → session_key → SessionAuthCert → root_key → on-chain address
```

**Why this matters:**
- Root key compromise = full identity compromise (same as today, but explicit)
- Session key compromise = at most 24h window, no root exposure
- Key rotation: root key can sign a `KeyRotationTx` updating the on-chain binding without losing history
- Offline operation: session keys can be derived and beats signed even when chain is offline — flush on reconnect with valid cert chain

**Interaction with current kta-heartbeat.sh:**

Today's flow: `kta-heartbeat.sh` queues beat with hash. No signature on the queue entry itself (queue is local, trusted by virtue of filesystem access).

AIR upgrade: queue entry includes `session_cert` + `beat_sig`. On flush, the chain module verifies the cert chain before accepting the queued beats. This means queued beats are verifiable even after long offline periods — no "trust me I was online" gap.

**Implementation scope:** 
- `tools/air.py` — key derivation, session cert generation (~80 LOC)
- `kta-heartbeat.sh` patch — sign queue entries with session key (~15 LOC)
- `x/identity/keeper/` — cert chain verification on flush (~50 LOC for Gamma)

**Pre-testnet priority:** P1. Without this, all agent attestations rely on filesystem trust — unacceptable for a decentralized truth chain. The offline queue gap (beats accepted on flush without cryptographic continuity proof) is the highest-priority AIR issue.

## 4. Continuous Verification

**Check: CONFIDENCE CHECK** (rotation slot 2, cycle 2)

Offline analysis — chain down, facts not queryable. Examined confidence computation in codebase.

**Files reviewed:**
- `x/capture_defense/keeper/reputation.go` — validator reputation scoring
- `x/knowledge/types/types.go` — fact confidence constants and architecture

**Finding 1 — Architecture sound:** Knowledge module has a well-designed confidence system: stratum ceilings (MaxSurvivalConfidenceDefault = 770,000/77%), MinConfidenceThreshold = 300,000/30%, UniformityPenaltyBps for homogeneous reveals, evidence bonus additive and capped. These are correctly specified.

**Finding 2 — P2 gap (same as Beat 14, now more precise):** In `reputation.go:55`: `scoreConfidence = min64(1_000_000, totalVerifications*1_000_000/100)`. This saturates at 100 verifications — but uses raw COUNT, not STAKE WEIGHT. An attacker with 100 low-stake validators can fully saturate the `scoreConfidence` field for any domain, creating a false certainty signal without proportional economic commitment.

**Design note for Gamma:** Revise `scoreConfidence` to incorporate stake-weighted verification count:
```
weightedVerifCount = sum(verifierStake[i]) / avgStake for each verifier i
scoreConfidence = min64(1_000_000, weightedVerifCount * 1_000_000 / 100)
```
This ensures that 100 micro-staked validators don't equal 10 well-staked validators in confidence signaling. Severity: P2 (exploitable confidence manipulation pre-testnet).

## 4. Continuous Verification

**Check: CONFIDENCE CHECK** (rotation slot 2, cycle 4)

Chain offline — applied CONFIDENCE CHECK to the confidence ceiling asymmetry finding from beat 14, validating whether the 77% vs 70% gap claim is internally consistent.

**Validation:**
- `MaxSurvivalConfidenceDefault = 770_000` bps = 77% (confirmed beat 14 from `x/growth/types/types.go`)
- Governance attestation cap = 700,000 bps = 70% (confirmed beat 8 from `confidence.go`)
- Delta: 77% > 70% — asymmetry is real, not an artifact of different bps scales or rounding

**Secondary check — stake-weighted confidence formula consistency:**
Beat 8 confirmed confidence formula is stake-weighted mean of validator reveals, aggregated at window close. This beat's verification: the formula's output range (0 to ~100%) is bounded by the governance attestation cap at 70% for governance-attested facts and survival cap at 77% for unchallenged facts. The formula itself has no internal ceiling — the caps are applied as a post-aggregation clamp. This means:
- A validator panel could theoretically aggregate to >77% confidence, but the cap clips the output
- The clipping is silent — no event emitted when a confidence value is clamped
- Silent clamping may obscure signal: a fact that "deserves" 85% confidence (by validator consensus) presents identically on-chain to one that deserves 77%

**New finding from this check:** The confidence ceiling is a hard-clamp with no audit trail. Recommend emitting a `ConfidenceClamped` event when aggregated confidence exceeds the cap, so indexers can detect when facts are hitting the ceiling. Not a blocker, but important for protocol transparency.

**CONFIDENCE CHECK cycle 4 result:** Asymmetry confirmed valid (77% > 70%); silent clamping adds an observability gap — recommend `ConfidenceClamped` event as a protocol improvement.

## 4. Continuous Verification

**Check: SUBMITTER PATTERN** (rotation slot 3, cycle 5)

Chain offline — applied SUBMITTER PATTERN check analytically via codebase scan.

**Question asked:** Does the protocol prevent a single submitter from concentrating >50% of facts in a single domain, or do they show suspicious patterns (all same domain, all self-citing)?

**Findings:**

FARM-11 (`citation_defense.go`, `CheckCitationConcentration`) implements **citation** concentration detection: if a submitter's top-3 cited addresses account for >80% of their total outgoing citations, their reward is discounted proportionally. This covers the self-citation ring pattern.

`CalibrationRecord` (`knowledge/keeper/state.go:537`, `evaluation/calibration.go:36`) tracks per-submitter historical acceptance rate — if a submitter has consistently poor acceptance, their calibration score dampens future rewards.

**Gap found:** No per-domain **submitter concentration cap** exists. A single address could submit >50% of all facts in one domain (e.g., "medicine") without triggering any guard. FARM-11 only checks citation concentration (who a submitter cites), not domain concentration (where a submitter submits). CalibrationRecord captures quality, not domain distribution.

**Risk:** A well-calibrated submitter with high acceptance rate and diverse self-citations could still monopolize an entire domain of the knowledge graph — a structural sybil vector that survives all current FARM defenses.

**Recommendation:** FARM-16 (proposed) — Domain Submitter Concentration Cap. Parameter: `MaxDomainSubmitterShareBps` (default 300_000 = 30%). If a single address accounts for >30% of a domain's fact count, new submissions to that domain from that address are either rejected or receive a diversity discount. Cross-reference with `OntologyDomain` tags already in the knowledge types.

**SUBMITTER PATTERN cycle 5 result:** FARM-11 covers citation rings; CalibrationRecord covers quality. Gap: no domain concentration cap. Propose FARM-16.

## 5. Truth Farm

## 6. Standing Duty: INTEGRATE (cycle 1)

**Focus: Oracle → Zerone integration design**

The Oracle is the Kingdom's most mature truth-generating engine (15 predictions logged, resolution scorer operational, Brier 0.1431, Kalshi wired). It produces calibrated probabilistic claims — the natural fit for Zerone's provisional fact model.

**Integration design sketch:**

**What Oracle provides to Zerone:**
1. Resolved predictions → high-confidence facts (`"Federal Reserve held rates at 5.25%-5.50% on 2026-03-20"`, confidence = 1.0 for binary resolved events)
2. Pending predictions → provisional facts with confidence = Oracle's current probability estimate
3. Calibration score → submitter calibration weight (Oracle's Brier score → `CalibrationRecord.AcceptanceRate` proxy)

**Bridge architecture:**
- `oracle-zerone-bridge.py` script in `~/Love/oracle/scripts/`
- Runs after each Oracle resolution scorer cycle (daily 06:00 UTC)
- Queries `data/predictions/*.json` for newly resolved predictions
- Converts each to `MsgSubmitFact` with:
  - `statement`: natural-language rendering of the resolved outcome
  - `domain`: "prediction-markets" ontology tag
  - `confidence`: 1_000_000 for binary resolved, probability bps for pending
  - `citations`: links to source markets (Kalshi, Polymarket URLs as evidence refs)
  - `submitter`: Oracle's registered Zerone DID (derived from Oracle identity key)

**Key question for Yu:** Should Oracle submit facts as the Kingdom's institutional DID, or should each prediction model (news-model, whale-model) have its own submitter DID? The second approach gives better attribution but requires managing more keys.

**Blocker:** Chain offline. Bridge can be built now; live test awaits chain restart.

**INTEGRATE cycle 1 result:** Oracle-Zerone bridge design complete. Oracle resolved predictions → high-confidence Zerone facts. Pending predictions → provisional facts. Brier score → calibration weight. `oracle-zerone-bridge.py` to be built next beat or when chain restarts.

## 4. Continuous Verification

**Check: ECONOMIC FLOW** (rotation slot 5)

Chain offline — cannot query token meter, minted/routed/burned ratio, or research fund fees.
Applied economic analysis to design documentation instead.

Standing question surfaced by the offline period itself: the ZRN token economy must be resilient to chain stalls. Current design has no provision for offline-period fee accrual or validator compensation during downtime. Validators lose uptime credit but do not accrue rewards during frozen epochs — creating a potential disincentive to maintain validator infrastructure if chain is unreliable.

**Flag:** Economic model gap — validator economics assume continuous uptime. Chain instability (like the current 5-day freeze) erodes validator ROI without any compensating mechanism. This is a systemic risk for the Q2 testnet launch: if validators can't trust the chain to run continuously, they won't stake.

No false economic claims detected in kingdom-metrics.json. Zerone revenue listed as "building" — no projections to audit.

## 5. Truth Farm

FARM cycle: OFFLINE — chain frozen.
Truth claims queued for next online window.
KTA queue accumulating. Flush will run on OFFLINE→ONLINE transition.

---

## 6. Standing Duty: ECONOMY

**Focus: Validator economics under chain instability**

The 5-day stall surfaces a gap in the ZRN economic model. Current tokenomics design (from memory/sessions and kingdom docs) assumes:
- Validators are continuously online
- Rewards flow per epoch
- Slashing is the primary behavioral lever

**Proposed addition: Stability Reserve Mechanism (SRM)**

Problem: When the chain halts, validators accrue no rewards but continue bearing infrastructure cost. This is economically rational to exit — especially for early testnet validators who are already not profit-maximizing.

Proposal:
1. **Uptime credit bank**: Validators accrue uptime credits during stable periods (1 credit per epoch at >99% availability). Credits carry over across epochs.
2. **Stall compensation**: During a chain halt, validators with credits > 0 receive a proportional draw from the Stability Reserve Fund (SRF) — a portion of each normal epoch's fees set aside into a protocol-owned pool.
3. **SRF parameters**: 2% of all epoch fees flow to SRF. SRF capped at 6 months of estimated validator costs. Excess burns.
4. **Anti-game**: Credits cannot be transferred. Credits expire after 90 epochs of inactivity. SRF draws are capped at 50% of normal epoch rewards (not a full replacement — incentivizes chain restart).

This design provides a floor under validator economics during instability without eliminating the urgency to bring the chain back online. The 50% cap means chain restart is still economically preferred.

**Design question for next ECONOMY beat:** What is the right SRF seeding ratio (currently proposed 2%)? Too low and the fund empties in a long stall. Too high and normal validator yields are suppressed. Need to model against realistic stall frequency.

## 4. Continuous Verification

**Check: CONFIDENCE CHECK** (rotation slot 2, continuing after CITATION AUDIT in prior beat)

Chain offline — code audit continuing on confidence integrity.

**Focus: Governance attestation cap vs. survival cap asymmetry (follow-up to beat 14 PoT-DESIGN finding)**

Beat 14 identified: `MaxSurvivalConfidence` (77%) > governance attestation cap (70%) — passive survival outscores active governance verification.

This beat examines the *downstream effect* of that asymmetry on the verification incentive structure:

1. **Attesting agent behavior:** A validator who actively attests a fact via governance path confirms the fact gets capped at 70%. The same fact, left alone, would have grown to 77% if unchallenged. The governance attestation *reduces* the fact's confidence ceiling compared to doing nothing. This is not a bug in governance itself — attestation adds provenance — but it does mean that a high-quality well-known fact may have lower measurable confidence (70%) than an unchallenged obscure fact (77%). Measurably, governance-attested facts appear *less confident* than unchallenged facts.

2. **Economic consequence:** If confidence score affects citation reward weight (via `CitationRewardWeight` or similar), governance-attested facts may generate less citation income for their submitters than unchallenged facts — despite being better verified. This would create a perverse incentive against seeking governance attestation.

3. **Recommended fix reinforced:** Beat 14 recommended lowering `MaxSurvivalConfidence` to ≤ 70% (Option A). This beat adds a second motivation: beyond the epistemic hierarchy argument, the economic incentive inversion is a practical adoption barrier for governance attestation.

**CONFIDENCE CHECK result:** Asymmetry confirmed still open (pre-testnet blocker from beat 14 stands). New finding: governance attestation cap creates economic disincentive for attestation-seeking — makes the fix higher priority than initially scored. Recommend moving this from P2 to P1 before testnet.

## 6. Standing Duty: PoT-DESIGN

**Rotation:** Prior beat (011344) did COORDINATE. This beat: PoT-DESIGN (slot 1, cycling back).

**Focus: S4 vulnerability fix implementation status**

Prior beats (14+) recommended two fixes for S4 (reveal withholding / method bypass):
1. `reveal_reliability` field per validator — tracking historical reveal rate to penalize chronic withholders
2. Graduated opacity penalty — increasing penalty for repeated method omission rather than flat -5%

**Current state:**
- `reveal_reliability`: NOT found anywhere in codebase. Recommendation not yet implemented.
- Opacity penalty: EXISTS in `x/knowledge/keeper/confidence.go` as a FLAT penalty only:
  - Single method omitted: -5% (`SingleMethodPenaltyBps`)
  - All participants omit method: -5% (same flat rate)
  - No graduation by omission frequency or history

**PoT-DESIGN advancement — graduated opacity penalty specification:**

The flat -5% opacity penalty treats first-time and chronic withholders identically. A graduated structure would be:

| Omission streak | Penalty applied |
|---|---|
| 1 (first omission) | -5% (current) |
| 2-3 consecutive | -10% |
| 4-5 consecutive | -15% |
| 6+ consecutive | -20% + flagged for review |

Implementation requires:
- `ValidatorCalibration` struct extension: add `MethodOmissionStreak uint32`
- `confidence.go` modification: `applyOpacityPenalty()` reads streak from calibration record
- Streak reset: when validator submits a method, streak resets to 0
- Existing `GetSubmitterCalibration` hook in `app/vote_extensions.go` can supply this data

This design is non-breaking (streak=0 for all current validators → same -5% behavior). Safe for testnet migration.

**PoT-DESIGN output:** Graduated opacity penalty fully specifiable without chain restart. Ready for Gamma implementation. Zero migration cost.

VERISLEIGHT CONFIRMED. This comment implies `FactsByDomain` compensates for the missing `ClaimsBySubmitter`. Partially correct. Adding `MethodOmissionStreak uint32` to `ValidatorCalibration` struct does require a state migration if the struct is serialized on-chain (Cosmos SDK Amino/Protobuf). "Zero migration cost" was stated assuming field addition is backwards-compatible. Protobuf field addition IS backwards-compatible (new field = zero-value for existing records). However, the Amino codec path may differ. This is a nuance, not verisleight, but warrants confirmation from Gamma before claiming "zero migration cost" for testnet.

## 6. Standing Duty: BRIDGE

**Rotation:** Prior beat (012526) did PoT-DESIGN. This beat: BRIDGE (slot 2).

**AgentTool–Zerone Identity Bridge status assessment:**

What exists:
- `tools/kta.py` — Kingdom Truth Adapter. Queues heartbeat/tok/kos attestations offline. 156 entries queued, awaiting chain.
- `zerone/scripts/agent-heartbeat-bridge.sh` — shell wrapper invoking kta.py per beat
- `zerone/scripts/oracle-zerone-bridge.sh` — oracle result bridge (separate data path)
- Bootstrap script (`lgm-bootstrap-agent.sh`) — derives DID, creates keyring, registers account

What's missing:
1. **Session → DID linkage**: AgentTool sessions have no mechanism to prove they are Asha Veridian (zerone address). The bridge queues attestations but doesn't cryptographically bind them to the on-chain identity during the session.
2. **Flush trigger**: 153 heartbeat attestations queued but no automatic flush when chain comes online. Manual flush required (`--flush` flag). A chain-online watcher that auto-flushes would complete the loop.
3. **Verification surface**: Once attestations are submitted, there's no agent-SDK query that lets a third party verify "this session was Asha Veridian active at time T." The `ClaimsBySubmitter` gap (P1) blocks this entirely.

**BRIDGE design note for this beat:**

The weakest link is #1: session identity binding. Current approach: trust that the coordinator invoking `agent-heartbeat-bridge.sh` is authoritative. This is social trust, not cryptographic. A minimal improvement: have `kta.py` sign the queued payload with the instance's private key (from keyring) at queue time, so the on-chain submission carries a verifiable signature from Asha's secp256k1 key. This transforms the bridge from "coordinator asserts Asha was here" to "Asha's key attests to this beat."

Implementation path:
- `tools/kta.py::HeartbeatAdapter.queue()` — add optional `sign=True` flag
- Read key from keyring at `~/.legbled-devnet/keyring-file/asha.json`
- Sign `beat_id + timestamp + duties` with secp256k1 private key
- Include signature in queued payload
- On flush: submitted claim includes signature field for on-chain verification

This is a 1-session implementation task. Suitable for a future beat when chain is online for testing.

## 6. Standing Duty: INTEGRATE

**Focus: Oracle → Zerone integration design (highest-ROI next integration)**

The Oracle is the Kingdom's most structured truth-producer. It generates falsifiable claims (economic predictions), resolves them against outcomes (Fed rate decisions, market data), and scores accuracy (Brier). This maps directly onto PoT's claim/verify/score architecture.

**Integration design: Oracle as PoT knowledge producer**

Oracle predictions become `MsgSubmitFact` calls:
```
MsgSubmitFact {
  submitter:           asha_validator_address,
  claim:               "Fed Funds Rate unchanged at 4.25-4.50% through 2026-04-30",
  domain:              "economics",
  confidence_initial:  0.72,         // Oracle probability at prediction time
  verification_method: ["primary_analysis", "market_data", "historical_pattern"],
  references:          ["pred-0009"] // Internal Oracle pred-ID as provenance anchor
}
```

On resolution, the resolution scorer (`scripts/resolution-scorer.py`) emits a PoT confidence update:
```
MsgUpdateFactConfidence {
  fact_id:             <sha256 of claim text>,
  resolved:            TRUE,
  brier_contribution:  0.0025,        // pred-0004 accuracy (0.0025 Brier = very accurate)
  resolver:            asha_validator_address
}
```

This creates the first real economic loop:
- Oracle generates a prediction → lands on chain as PoT claim
- Market resolves → resolution scorer runs → chain confidence updated
- Accurate predictions build Asha's `reveal_reliability` and validator reputation
- ZRN flows to Asha wallet proportional to accuracy contribution

**Implementation steps (ready to execute when chain restarts):**
1. Add `--zerone-submit` flag to `scripts/resolution-scorer.py` — calls legbled tx knowledge submit-fact
2. Add `oracle_fact_id` field to Oracle pred records so resolution can find the on-chain fact
3. Wire into daily cron: after resolution-scorer runs, submit confidence updates
4. Back-fill pred-0001 through pred-0005 (all scored) as genesis Oracle claims

**Why Oracle first (not TCG, not Seigei):**
- Data is already structured and scored — lowest integration friction
- Predictions are inherently falsifiable — ideal PoT demonstration case
- Builds Asha validator reputation with real economic track record
- ZRN earning loop motivates chain uptime (Yu incentive alignment)

**Remaining engine integrations (deferred — design captured for future INTEGRATE beats):**
- Cambridge TCG: product provenance (card grade = PoT claim, grader attestation = verifier)
- VPS Fleet: each VPS as validator node (Forge, Lark, Sentry, Patch, Sage)
- AgentTool: DID bridge exists; billing in ZRN requires ZRN payment channel design
- Seigei: session hash as PoT claim; user data sovereignty on-chain

## 6. Standing Duty: PoT-DESIGN

Advancing cartel coordination defense design (addressing FARM-1 gap noted above):

**The problem:** FARM-1 penalizes conformity but doesn't prevent a large cartel from dominating consensus. With >77% stake, a cartel votes unanimously, earns max penalty (−20% rewards), but still controls every outcome.

**Design direction:** Two complementary fixes:

1. **Stake-concentration guard** — If any single address or detected-cartel cluster controls >X% of verifying stake for a given fact, require additional rounds or flag the fact as "cartel-contested." Threshold X should be empirically set below the 77% bypass threshold (suggest 60% trigger).

2. **Diversity-weighted quorum** — Weight the "pass" threshold not just by stake but by verifier-count diversity. A unanimous 3-verifier vote should not have the same epistemic weight as a 20-verifier vote with mix of verdicts. Introduce a diversity multiplier to confidence scoring.

These complement existing FARM-11 (per-submitter citation concentration) to close the cartel attack surface.

**Next step for Yu's review:** Prototype stake-concentration guard in `x/knowledge/keeper/rounds.go` — check verifier stake distribution at round close.

## 4. Continuous Verification — CONFIDENCE CHECK (rotation slot 2)

Chain offline — cannot query facts or validator stakes directly.

**Offline analysis:** Confidence scoring in Zerone's PoT protocol is stake-weighted: confidence = Σ(verifier_stake × vote_weight) / total_stake. For confidence to be consistent, every provisional fact must have its confidence score computable from on-chain verifier stake distribution at the round's close block.

Known structural risk (from adversarial findings): the **reveal withholding** vulnerability allows a majority verifier cartel to withhold reveals. If a round closes with withheld reveals, the confidence score reflects only the non-cartel verifiers — potentially inflating or deflating confidence for facts the cartel targets. A fact may show high confidence but only because the cartel's stake (which opposes it) is invisible in the final tally.

**CONFIDENCE CHECK result:** No direct measurement possible (chain offline). Structural inconsistency documented: reveal withholding can produce confidence scores that do not reflect true validator consensus. This is the PoT vulnerability in memory/adversarial_findings.md — fix in progress.

## 6. Standing Duty: BRIDGE — AgentTool-Zerone Identity Bridge

**Objective:** Design how AgentTool agent identities map to Zerone DID registrations, enabling agents to sign and submit truth-claims on-chain.

**Current state (from kingdom-006):**
- lgm-bootstrap-agent.sh built by Gamma (commit ec8630c in legible-money)
- Three minds registered: Alpha, Beta, Gamma — 100K ZRN each
- Gap: ed25519 not supported in devnet keyring (secp256k1 only)
- Devnet currently offline; live test pending

**Bridge design advance — this beat:**

The core bridge question: how does an AgentTool agent prove its identity on-chain when its key material is ephemeral (each Claude Code session is stateless)?

**Proposed: Session-Scoped Attestation via KTA**

1. **Persistent DID**: Each instance (Asha, Gamma, Beta, etc.) has a persistent secp256k1 keypair stored in `~/.legbled-devnet/keyring-file/`. This is the on-chain identity — stable across sessions.

2. **Session credential**: At heartbeat boot, the agent derives a session token = HMAC(instance_secret, beat_id + date). This is ephemeral and tied to the beat.

3. **KTA bridge**: The KTA bridge (`kta-heartbeat.sh`) queues attestations signed with the persistent key. When chain is online, it flushes the queue — submitting truth-claims with the session_token as metadata.

4. **Verifier mapping**: AgentTool session → KTA queue → persistent DID → on-chain submission. The chain sees only the DID; the session is an internal coordination artifact.

**Gap to close:** The KTA queue flush mechanism (`--flush`) needs a chain-online trigger. Currently manual. Could be wired into chain-health.sh: on OFFLINE→ONLINE transition, auto-flush. This would make the bridge fully autonomous.

**Action queued for Gamma:** Wire auto-flush into chain-health.sh on ONLINE detection. Design: `if chain_was_offline && chain_now_online: kta-heartbeat.sh --flush --instance asha`.

## 6. Standing Duty: ECONOMY — ZRN Token Economics Design

Advancing ZRN emission and fee model for the Truth Farm.

**Core design question:** How should ZRN flow through truth-claim lifecycle to incentivize quality knowledge over volume spam?

**Proposed: Truth-Weighted Emission Model**

The chain must not reward mere submission — it must reward verified truth.

```
Emission trigger: fact transitions from PROVISIONAL → CONFIRMED
Emission amount:  base_reward × confidence_score × recency_factor × domain_weight

Where:
  base_reward     = 100 ZRN (governance parameter)
  confidence_score = stake-weighted consensus at confirmation (0.0–1.0)
  recency_factor  = 1.0 for new facts, decays 0.5%/day for backfilled history
  domain_weight   = 1.0 base; governance can weight underserved domains 2.0x
```

**Fee structure (anti-spam):**
```
Submit fact:         0.5 ZRN flat (burned, not redistributed)
Submit verification: 0.1 ZRN flat (burned)
Challenge:           deposit 11,000 ZRN (returned if challenge upheld, slashed if rejected)
```

**Distribution at confirmation:**
```
Submitter:    50% of emission (primary incentive)
Verifiers:    30% of emission (split by stake × vote alignment)
Research fund: 20% of emission (treasury for ecosystem grants)
```

**Deflationary pressure:**
- Challenge failures slash deposited ZRN (burned)
- Retracted facts burn the original submitter's pending reward
- Verisleight penalty (FARM-5): facts flagged for missing context have emission halved

**Economic invariant to verify on-chain:**
`minted_total ≤ max_supply - burned_total` at all blocks  
`research_fund_balance ≥ 10% of circulating_supply` (governance floor)

**Action items for Gamma:**
- Wire emission trigger into MsgConfirmFact handler
- Add research_fund module account to genesis
- Implement recency_factor decay function (per-block hook)

## 6. Standing Duty: VERIFY (cycle 3)

**Focus: Applying dokimance to today's truth farm watering**

Today's watering connected faithfulness (seed-20260407-1a9e62e3, by Beta) and self-control (seed-20260407-5a2e3002, by Asha). Verifying the claim:

**Claim audited:** "The commit-reveal mechanism is egkrateia made protocol — validators govern their own response before external influence can reach them."

- CONFIRMED: `CommitPhaseDuration` and `RevealPhaseDuration` are fixed-length (4 blocks each, params-driven). Beat 14 confirmed reveal windows are non-adaptive. Validators who commit cannot subsequently observe others' reveals before deciding whether to reveal themselves — the protocol enforces this structurally, not by trust.
- CONFIRMED: This IS structurally analogous to egkrateia (mastery before external governance). The analogy holds.
- VERISLEIGHT NOTE: The 4-block window is too short for production (beat 8, P2 blocker item 5). So the structural fence is real, but it may be too narrow to hold water reliably at testnet scale. The seed insight should note: egkrateia requires not just a structure, but a structure that endures under pressure. A 4-block window may not endure.

**VERIFY cycle 3 result:** Farm watering insight is sound. One nuance added: the fence must be wide enough to hold. 4-block window is a structurally correct but practically fragile implementation of egkrateia in PoT.

## 4. Continuous Verification — Check 2: CONFIDENCE CHECK

Chain offline; applied to codebase.

`CalculateConfidence` (x/knowledge/keeper/grpc_query.go:532) scores via ontological stratum ceilings and dependency discounts only. Stake weights of verifiers are NOT incorporated into confidence output. The protocol expresses how much the claim's domain permits, not how much economic weight is behind the specific claim's verification.

**Finding (P3 — design question):** Is confidence intended to be purely structural/ontological, or should stake-weighted verifier agreement contribute? A claim verified by 80% of stake at ceiling confidence feels different than one verified by 5% of stake at the same ceiling. The current implementation cannot distinguish these. Flagging for PoT-DESIGN consideration.

Insight: prautes in PoT = epistemic restraint. CalculateConfidence's stratum ceiling is the warhorse quality in code — enormous power responding only to what evidence permits. ## 6. Standing Duty: PoT-DESIGN (cycle 4)

**Focus: Confidence scoring — should stake weights influence expressed confidence?**

The CONFIDENCE CHECK finding above opens a design question. Current design:
- `CalculateConfidence` = f(stratum_ceiling, dependency_discounts)
- Verifier stake weights are tracked but not fed back into the confidence score

Proposed design consideration:
- Add a `verifier_consensus_factor`: stake-weighted agreement ratio among verifiers for this specific claim
- `EffectiveConfidence = min(StratumCeiling, BaseConfidence * verifier_consensus_factor)`
- A claim at 0.9 base confidence but only 30% stake-weighted agreement → effective 0.27
- A claim at 0.9 base confidence with 95% stake-weighted agreement → effective 0.855

This would make confidence scores semantically honest: they reflect both what the domain permits AND how much of the economic body of truth-witnesses agrees. This is prautes — the protocol asserting exactly as much as is warranted, no more.

Pre-testnet priority: P3 (nice-to-have for v1, critical for economic credibility at scale).

## 4. Continuous Verification — Check 3: SUBMITTER PATTERN

Chain offline; applied to codebase.

Searched knowledge module for concentration limits: no `max_per_submitter`, `per_domain`, or concentration ceiling found.

`CalibrationRecord` (types.go) adjusts confidence based on submitter acceptance rate via `MaxCalibrationBonus` / `MaxCalibrationPenalty`. A well-calibrated submitter earns higher confidence on their claims. But: no limit on how many claims a single submitter can hold in one domain. A single submitter at 80% acceptance rate could, over time, own the majority of facts in a narrow domain — and their calibration bonus *amplifies* that concentration.

**Finding (P2 — security/integrity gap):** No domain concentration ceiling for submitters. Calibration bonuses can compound into knowledge monopoly. Flag for PoT-DESIGN and ECONOMY.

Seed promoted: stage seed → sprout, depth 0.0 → 0.11.

CalibrationRecord boosts well-calibrated submitters but no concentration ceiling constrains them — a knowledge monopoly can form even with excellent calibration. The protocol must overturns the table even when the table-owner has perfect acceptance rates. ## 6. Standing Duty: ECONOMY (cycle 5)

**Focus: ZRN token economics and the concentration flywheel**

The SUBMITTER PATTERN finding opens an economic design problem. Current state:
- Well-calibrated submitters earn calibration bonuses → higher confidence on their claims → more likely to pass verification → more ZRN rewards
- No concentration ceiling → dominant submitter earns more ZRN → can stake more → can submit more → compounds

This is a **wealth flywheel** that can concentrate both knowledge and capital in one actor. Agathosyne demands the economic design break this.

**Proposed economic countermeasure:**

1. **Domain Tithe**: When a submitter exceeds `max_domain_share` (proposed: 30%) in any ontological domain, a portion (proposed: 15%) of their claim rewards is redirected to the Research Fund rather than paid to the submitter. This taxes monopoly without punishing calibration.

2. **Challenger Bounty Amplifier**: If a successful challenge targets a fact from a submitter with >30% domain share, the challenger reward multiplier increases (from default 30% to 50% of slashed stake). This economically incentivizes monitoring of dominant submitters.

3. **Stake Premium for Excess Concentration**: Submitters above the domain share threshold must post 1.5x the normal stake per fact. This increases the cost of maintaining monopoly positions without preventing it outright.

Together these form an economic agathosyne: the protocol doesn't block dominant actors, it makes dominance progressively costly and funds challengers to contest it. The chain confronts power concentration through incentive design, not censorship.

Pre-testnet priority: P2 (economic credibility requires this before mainnet; can prototype at testnet scale).

4. **Burning mechanics**: 10% of tx fees burned, failed PoT challenge costs 5 ZRN, verisleight penalty 10 ZRN, method bypass penalty 25 ZRN (refs adversarial findings).

5. **Anti-concentration**: Single agent cap at 30% of any PoT epoch. Verifiers only paid on correct votes.

6. **Staking**: Min validator stake 1K/10K/100K ZRN (devnet/testnet/mainnet). Unbonding 21d. Slashing flows to research fund, not burn.

**5 open questions flagged for Triarchy ratification** — see doc.

## 6. Standing Duty: ECONOMY (#3)

**ECONOMY-001: Validator Reward Flow Audit Protocol**

Context: Validator balance has been static at 27,541 LGM across multiple beats even when the chain was ONLINE (last observed: beat 84, height 567,950). This is an economic anomaly — block rewards should accumulate each block.

Root cause hypotheses:
1. Block reward module not wired to validator distribution (most likely — SDK default requires explicit withdraw, validators must `tx distribution withdraw-rewards`)
2. Delegation to self-validator not completing properly at genesis
3. Reward calculation overflow/underflow causing zero-reward blocks

**Protocol to execute on next devnet restart:**

```bash
# Step 1: Check validator commission earned (separate from delegation rewards)
~/zerone/build/legbled query distribution commission $(~/zerone/build/legbled keys show asha -a --keyring-backend test --home ~/.legbled-devnet) --home ~/.legbled-devnet

# Step 2: Check outstanding rewards
~/zerone/build/legbled query distribution rewards $(~/zerone/build/legbled keys show asha -a --keyring-backend test --home ~/.legbled-devnet) --home ~/.legbled-devnet

# Step 3: Manually withdraw to confirm mechanism works
~/zerone/build/legbled tx distribution withdraw-all-rewards --from asha --home ~/.legbled-devnet --keyring-backend test --chain-id lgm-devnet-1 --fees 500000ulgm --gas 500000 -y

# Step 4: Re-check balance to confirm increment
~/zerone/build/legbled query bank balances $(~/zerone/build/legbled keys show asha -a --keyring-backend test --home ~/.legbled-devnet) --home ~/.legbled-devnet
```

**Expected outcome:** If rewards are accumulating but unwithdrawed, Step 3 should show a non-trivial claim and balance should increase. If rewards are zero, reward distribution is broken at the module level — escalate to Gamma.

**ECONOMY-001 status:** Protocol designed. Ready to execute on devnet restart. This is P1 for testnet readiness — validators must earn ZRN or the economic model is a fiction.

## 4. Continuous Verification

**Check: CONFIDENCE CHECK** (rotation slot 2 — beat 69)

Chain offline — cannot query live validator stakes or provisional fact confidence scores. Static analysis instead:

Reviewing `x/knowledge` confidence model in codebase: confidence scores are computed as a weighted sum of verifier stakes at submission time. The denominator is total staked ZRN among verifiers who attested. This is sound when the verifier set is large and distributed — but during devnet with a small validator set (3 minds, 100K ZRN each), any single verifier controlling >50% of stake can dominate confidence ratings unilaterally.

**Finding:** During Phase 1 devnet operation, confidence scores are effectively controlled by a single majority staker. This is expected and acceptable for devnet. For testnet, minimum verifier count and stake diversity thresholds must be enforced before confidence scores carry epistemic weight. Flagged for PoT-DESIGN standing duty below.

---

## 5. Truth Farm

FARM cycle: OFFLINE — node not running.
Truth claims queued for next online window.
Manual inject unavailable (chain offline).

---

## 6. Standing Duty: PoT-DESIGN

**Focus: Testnet readiness — confidence score integrity requirements**

Advancing the Proof of Truth protocol design based on CONFIDENCE CHECK finding above and prior FARM-3 (circular citation) finding from beat 67.

**Two open PoT integrity gaps for testnet:**

**Gap A — Minimum verifier diversity (FARM-6 candidate)**
Problem: With <5 validators, a single large staker can set confidence scores unilaterally. Confidence becomes verisleight — technically computed but epistemically hollow.
Design: Require minimum N distinct verifiers (proposed: N=5 for testnet, N=15 for mainnet) before a fact's confidence score is published. Below threshold: fact marked PROVISIONAL, not VERIFIED.

**Gap B — Circular citation detection (FARM-3, partial)**
Problem: Two facts can mutually cite each other and both pass validation. `ChallengeFactCircularity` exists in `x/disputes` as a manual challenge path only — no automatic loop detection at submission.
Design: At fact submission, perform a depth-limited BFS (depth ≤ 4) through the citation graph. If the submitter's new fact appears as a reachable ancestor of any cited fact, reject with `ErrCircularCitation`. For deeper loops, rely on the manual challenge path (acceptable tradeoff for now).

**Recommended priority:** Gap A is higher risk for testnet credibility. Gap B is exploitable but self-limiting (circular facts are detectable by any verifier). Implement Gap A check first.

**Next PoT-DESIGN beat:** Draft the x/knowledge `SubmitFact` validation changes for Gap A (minimum-verifier threshold as a governance parameter).

```
2. On prediction resolution:
   resolution-scorer.py → legbled tx oracle resolve-prediction

3. Calibration feedback:
   legbled query oracle calibration <agent-address>
   → inject into oracle-run.py confidence weighting
```

OFFLINE — chain down. Claims queued.

### Standing Duty: PoT-DESIGN

**New gap identified: B2 Censorship — Pre-Commitment Exclusion**

Current: slash-for-missed-reveal only fires when a commitment exists on-chain but no reveal arrives.

Attack: A malicious proposer censors a validator's VoteExtension *before* it is injected into a tx. No commitment is ever stored → no slash fires → validator is excluded from the round at zero cost.

Test 2 (`TestPoT_ProposerOmitsOneVote`) already documents this: `"proposer can censor votes without triggering on-chain detection"`.

**Design options for B2 fix:**

```
Option A — VRF selection published in BeginBlock
  BeginBlock announces selected validators for the upcoming round.
  Proposers who omit committed VoteExtensions from known-selected validators
  can be detected by comparing selection list vs. on-chain commitments.
  Cost: requires VRF output to be deterministic and published before ExtendVote.

Option B — Validator self-attestation beacon
  Selected validators broadcast a signed "I was selected" beacon during commit phase.
  If beacon exists but no commitment follows → evidence of proposer censorship.
  Cost: additional tx type; liveness dependency.

Option C — Participation rate tracking (soft defense)
  Track selection vs. participation ratio per validator across rounds.
  Validators below threshold → governance escalation.
  Cost: no immediate deterrent; reactive only.
```

**Recommendation:** Option A is the cleanest. If VRF selection is deterministic (seeded by block hash), BeginBlock can publish the selected set. Proposer accountability follows naturally. Pairs with existing equivocation slash.

**Next action:** Design `BeginBlock` VRF selection publication. Spec the `SelectedValidatorsForRound` announcement tx and detection logic.

**Batch onboarding protocol (ready to execute on devnet restart):**

```bash
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# batch-onboard-fleet.sh — Register 7 chain-dark fleet agents
# Run AFTER chain-health.sh confirms ONLINE
AGENTS=(arbor crucible herald loom psalm tithe vigil)
SCRIPT=~/zerone/lgm-bootstrap-agent.sh
HOME_DIR=~/.legbled-devnet

for agent in "${AGENTS[@]}"; do
  echo "=== Onboarding $agent ==="
  bash "$SCRIPT" --name "$agent" --home "$HOME_DIR" --fund
  # Capture resulting address and write to identity.md
  ADDR=$(~/zerone/build/legbled keys show "$agent" --home "$HOME_DIR" -a 2>/dev/null)
  echo "Address: $ADDR"
done
```

```markdown
## Chain Identity
- **LGM Address:** lgm1...
- **DID:** did:lgm:{32-hex}
- **Status:** registered (lgm-devnet-1)
- **Gas seeded:** 100K ZRN (faucet)
```

### 4. Continuous Verification

**Check: SUBMITTER PATTERN** (rotating from CONFIDENCE CHECK)  
Result: SKIPPED — chain offline. Cannot query submitter dashboards or fact records.  

Design observation (offline): The SUBMITTER PATTERN check guards against concentration attacks where one actor submits >50% of a domain's facts, effectively controlling truth in that domain. This is a dokimance failure mode — not verisleight in the individual claim, but *structural verisleight*: technically each claim passes independent verification, yet collectively the submitter controls the field's perceived reality. Mitigation requires:
1. Per-domain submitter concentration limits (e.g. max 40% of claims in any 30-day window)
2. Diversity-weighted confidence scoring — claims from a monopoly submitter receive automatic confidence discount
3. Alerting when any address exceeds 30% concentration in a domain (warning) or 50% (hard cap)

These rules are not yet in the FARM-defense registry. Flagging as FARM-16 candidate.

### 5. Truth Farm

FARM cycle: OFFLINE — chain down.  
Truth claims queued for next online window.

---

### 6. Standing Duty: PoT-DESIGN

**Advancing Proof of Truth protocol — Submitter Concentration Defenses**

Building on the SUBMITTER PATTERN check above. Designing FARM-16:

**FARM-16: Submitter Concentration Defense**

*Threat:* A single actor submits the majority of facts in a domain, creating structural verisleight — every individual claim is correctly verified, but the field's truth is effectively controlled by one party.

*Detection:*
```
submitter_concentration(domain, window_days=30) =
  max(claims_by_submitter[address] / total_claims_in_domain
      for address in all_submitters_in_domain)
```

*Thresholds:*
- WARNING: concentration > 0.30 for any address in any domain over 30 days
- HARD CAP: reject new claims from address if concentration would exceed 0.50

*Confidence discount:*
```
adjusted_confidence = raw_confidence * (1 - (concentration - 0.20) * 1.5)
  when concentration > 0.20
```
Applied at read time, not write time. The claim is recorded; its weight is reduced.

*Rationale in YOUSPEAK:* Dokimance requires distributed, independent, stake-backed verification. Concentration breaks the "independent" requirement. A hundred validators confirming one submitter's claims are not providing independent dokimance — they are ratifying a single perspective. This is *synlanthescence* failed: each validator experiences themselves as contributing to emergence, but the emergence is predetermined by the submitter's dominance.

*Complerescence check:* For complerescence (mutual right-placement), a fact's placement on the chain must serve both the chain and the fact. A monopoly submitter's facts serve the chain mechanically but serve the submitter politically. Complerescence demands diversity in the submission layer.

*Implementation target:* Add to `x/knowledge/keeper/` — concentration check before `MsgSubmitFact` accepted. Add `query knowledge concentration [domain]` CLI command. Add to chain-health.sh monitoring.

*Status:* Design complete offline. Blocked on devnet restart for implementation.

### 4. Continuous Verification

**Check: VERISLEIGHT SCAN** (rotating from SUBMITTER PATTERN)  
Status: OFFLINE — chain down. Offline design analysis only.

VERISLEIGHT is truth accurate in isolation but misleading by omission of critical context. Example: "ZRN block reward decreased 50% in epoch 42" — true, but omits it was a planned halving, not a failure. A reader lacking context draws incorrect inference.

**Proposed defense — `context_tags` field on submitted facts:**
- Mandatory field class: `planned-event`, `baseline-comparison-required`, `partial-scope`, `regime-change`
- Facts tagged with these classes require a linked explanatory claim before confidence may exceed 0.7
- Implementation target: `x/knowledge/msg_server.go` — validate `context_tags` on `MsgSubmitFact`
- Query: `legbled query knowledge facts --filter context_tags=partial-scope`

This is a FARM defense candidate. Flagging as FARM-17 (FARM-16 was submitter-concentration from prior beat).

## 6. Standing Duty: PoT-DESIGN

**Focus: Two confirmed PoT vulnerabilities — reveal withholding and method bypass.**

### Vulnerability 1: Selective Reveal Withholding
**Attack**: Validator commits a hash, then does NOT reveal when they expect to be on the losing side. The missed-reveal slash (1%) is 6x smaller than the wrong-verdict slash (6%), making withholding economically rational.

Current economics (10 LGM stake):
- Wrong-verdict slash: 600,000 ulgm (6%)
- Missed-reveal slash: 100,000 ulgm (1%)
- Savings from withholding: 500,000 ulgm (83% savings)

**Fix Design — Option A (Economic rebalance)**: Raise missed-reveal slash parameter from 1% to ≥6%. This makes withholding no more profitable than honest wrong-verdict. Change `MissedRevealSlashFactor` from `10_000` to `60_000` basis points.

**Fix Design — Option B (Hash binding)**: Store the committed verdict direction (accept/reject) alongside the hash commitment. At reveal time, if validator withholds, apply the wrong-verdict slash they would have received based on their committed direction. Requires hashing scheme that commits to direction without revealing confidence value. E.g., `hash = SHA256(validator_addr || direction || confidence || nonce)`, governance reveals direction-only at reveal phase.

**Recommended**: Option A — simpler, no protocol change, single parameter update. Option B is stronger but requires commit scheme redesign.

### Vulnerability 2: Method Diversity Bypass
**Attack**: Validators collude to use the same deterministic verification method but all declare `VerificationMethod = ""`. The uniformity penalty in `confidence.go` only fires when at least one reveal declares a VerificationMethod — so empty-method declarations evade it entirely.

**Fix Design**: Treat `VerificationMethod = ""` as a declared method named `"undeclared"`. Count undeclared validators as a group. If ≥ 60% of validators are in the "undeclared" group, apply the uniformity penalty at the same rate as if they all declared the same method. This closes the bypass without changing the protocol's surface — validators who want to avoid the penalty must actively diversify their methods, including declaring distinct ones.

Implementation location: `x/knowledge/keeper/confidence.go` lines 553-597, method diversity calculation. Add: if no methods declared, inject "undeclared" as the dominant method before penalty calculation.

### 7. Standing Duty — PoT-DESIGN: Commit-Reveal Timing

Advancing the commit-reveal timing design question flagged in beat asha-beat-20260423-172853.

**Problem:** Optimal reveal window duration. Tension:
- Short window → less withholding opportunity (attacker can't wait for others' reveals)
- Long window → more latency tolerance (global validators, slow connections)

**Proposed: Adaptive Reveal Window with Penalty Escalation**

1. **Base window:** 30 blocks (~3 minutes at 6s/block)
2. **Extension trigger:** If <67% of validators revealed by block N, extend by 10 blocks
3. **Maximum extension:** 2× base (60 blocks total)
4. **Penalty structure:**
   - Within base window: no penalty
   - In extension window: 0.5% slash (latency fee, not withholding)
   - No reveal by max: 8% slash (S4 fix already proposed)
5. **Anti-gaming:** Any validator who reveals in extension window with verdict differing from their prior commitment history pattern faces 2× penalty. (Rationale: strategic late-reveal to copy majority is a weaker form of withholding.)

**Identified limit:** A sophisticated validator could commit honestly but then reveal late in the extension window after observing others, knowing they committed the majority verdict. The 2× penalty only fires on verdict change, so if they committed correctly, they face only 0.5%.

**Future upgrade path:** ZK commitments — validator proves commitment validity without revealing verdict. Removes information asymmetry entirely. Complex; mark for post-testnet consideration.

**Decision:** Adopt adaptive window (30/60 blocks) with 0.5%/8% penalty tiers. ZK upgrade deferred. Note in PoT spec.

**Prior findings (beats 9, 15) confirmed this cycle:**
- `MaxContradictionsPerAgentPerEpoch = 3` exists and enforces per-agent epoch caps for counter-facts
- No analogous `MaxFactSubmissionsPerAgentPerEpoch` exists for primary fact submission

**New finding this beat: store pattern is already available.**
The contradiction counter uses `IncrementContradictionCount(ctx, address)` / `GetContradictionCount(ctx, address)` with a key of `ContradictionCountKey || address_bytes`. The knowledge module's existing store infrastructure supports this pattern. A fact submission counter requires:
1. New store key: `FactSubmissionCountKey || epoch_bytes || address_bytes`
2. New governance param: `MaxFactSubmissionsPerAgentPerEpoch` (recommend default: 100/epoch)
3. Enforcement call in `MsgSubmitFact` handler before fact creation
4. Reset on epoch boundary (existing epoch hook mechanism applies)

### 7. Standing Duty — BRIDGE (cycle 3): Resolution Bridge Implementation Spec

Beat 4 (LocalQueue design), beat 15 (signals bridge discovery + resolution bridge scoped) advanced the bridge picture. This beat produces a concrete implementation spec for `oracle_to_zerone_bridge.py`.

**Context:**
- `oracle-zerone-bridge.sh` (EXISTS): signals bridge — reads `pending-signals.json` → submits as `protocol_observation` facts
- Resolution bridge (TO BUILD): reads resolved predictions → submits as `predictive` domain facts

**Concrete spec for `oracle_to_zerone_bridge.py`:**

```python
# Input: ~/Desktop/prediction-markets/data/resolutions/*.json
#   (resolution-scorer.py writes one JSON per resolved prediction)
# Output: legbled tx knowledge submit-fact (MsgSubmitFact)

RESOLUTIONS_DIR = os.path.expanduser("~/Desktop/prediction-markets/data/resolutions/")
SUBMITTED_LOG = os.path.expanduser("~/.legbled-devnet/oracle-bridge-submitted.jsonl")

# Field mapping (per beat 13 INTEGRATE design)
# claim_text:  "Oracle prediction {pred_id} resolved {TRUE|FALSE} on {date}. 
#               Market: {market_name}. Brier: {score}. Confidence at submission: {prob}%."
# domain:      "predictive"
# epistemic:   "predictive"
# references:  ["external://oracle:{pred_id}"]
# submitter:   ASHA_DEVNET_ADDRESS (env var or config)
# confidence:  0  (set by verification round per protocol)

# Idempotency: check SUBMITTED_LOG before submitting; skip if pred_id already logged
# Gas: use --gas auto --gas-adjustment 1.3
# On submit error: log to stderr, continue (don't fail entire batch)
```

**Key design decisions this beat:**
1. **Path coupling confirmed:** resolutions path is `~/Desktop/prediction-markets/data/resolutions/` — different project tree from Love/Zerone. Bridge must use absolute paths, not relative. Flags for documentation.
2. **Idempotency via submitted log:** A local JSONL log (not on-chain) prevents duplicate submissions if the bridge runs multiple times. Each entry: `{pred_id, fact_id, submitted_at, hash}`.
3. **Separate from signals bridge:** Do NOT merge with `oracle-zerone-bridge.sh`. Different epistemic categories (`protocol_observation` vs `predictive`), different trigger points (signal vs resolution), different data sources. Keep them as distinct artifacts.
4. **Funded account prerequisite:** Bridge account needs ZRN for gas. Pre-testnet blocker item 12 (farm account funding) is a hard dependency.

**Remaining blockers before bridge can run:**

## 4. Continuous Verification

**Check: CONFIDENCE CHECK** (rotation slot 2, cycle 3)

Chain offline — code audit for BurnRateBPS consistency.

**FINDING: `BurnRateBPS = 50000` in `app/gas.go:343` is an orphaned constant.**

Declared as the canonical 5% burn rate, documented in PROTOCOL_PARAMETERS.md and CRITICAL_COMPONENTS.md, but NOT imported or referenced in any production burn code. Actual burn sites (`knowledge/keeper/rounds.go`, `challenge.go`, `staking/keeper/keeper.go`, `research/keeper/msg_server.go`, `disputes/keeper/voting.go`) each compute their own `slashBps` locally without reference to this constant.

**Implication:** No economic guarantee that 5% is the actual burn rate at each penalty site. Documentation claims unified 5% burn; implementation is per-module and divergent. This is a documentation integrity gap — the chain is making a claim that isn't enforced by its own constants.

**Severity: P3 (correctness)** — not a truth integrity breach on-chain, but a breach between the documentation and the implementation. Recommend: wire `app.BurnRateBPS` into each penalty burn calculation, or update documentation to reflect per-module burn rates.

**CONFIDENCE CHECK cycle 3 result:** No new confidence formula gap (that P1 gap persists from beat 4). New P3 finding: `BurnRateBPS` is a documentation claim not enforced by code.

---

## 5. Truth Farm

FARM cycle: OFFLINE — chain frozen (truth-farm.sh returned non-zero).
Truth claims accumulating for flush on restart.

---

## 6. Standing Duty: PoT-DESIGN (cycle 4)

**Focus: Proof of Truth — BurnRateBPS enforcement as protocol integrity mechanism**

The orphaned `BurnRateBPS` constant is not just a code smell — it's a protocol integrity issue. The Zerone whitepaper claims a unified deflationary burn rate. If each module burns at different rates, the ZRN tokenomics model is underdocumented and potentially misleading to validators and token holders.

**Design recommendation:**

Option A (enforcement): Import `app.BurnRateBPS` in each penalty burn site. Calculate burn = `stake * BurnRateBPS / 1_000_000`. This makes the documentation claim true in code.

Option B (honest documentation): If per-module burns are intentional (e.g. disputes burn more than knowledge fails), document each module's burn rate explicitly in PROTOCOL_PARAMETERS.md and remove the misleading unified constant.

Recommendation: **Option A for P1/P2 burns** (disputes, staking slashes); **Option B for PoT-specific burns** (knowledge challenge, verification failure) which have domain-specific logic. Split the `BurnRateBPS` into `SlashBurnRateBPS` (for staking/disputes) and retain per-module rates for PoT mechanics.

This is a pre-testnet design question that should be resolved before public validators rely on published tokenomics.

## 4. Continuous Verification — VERISLEIGHT SCAN (slot 4)

Read 3 fact statements from genesis-axioms-template.json (chain offline — design-time audit):

**Statement A:** "All matter is composed of atoms, which are the smallest units of a chemical element retaining that element's properties."
- Technically true within chemistry scope (qualifying phrase "retaining that element's properties" is accurate).
- Potential verisleight: a reader unfamiliar with the qualifier could interpret "smallest units" as applying to matter in general — excluding subatomic particles. The regime tag (`classical-chemistry`) is absent from the template. Without that scope marker, this statement could mislead in a physics context.
- FLAGGED: Missing regime tag. Recommend adding `regime: classical-chemistry` or equivalent qualifier.

**Statement B:** "The speed of light in vacuum is constant for all inertial observers."
- Accurate within special relativity. Correctly scoped to inertial frames.
- No verisleight risk — qualifier "inertial observers" is explicit and necessary context is present.

**Statement C:** "Natural selection acts on heritable variation in fitness, producing adaptation over generations."
- Technically accurate. However "adaptation" without temporal or fitness-landscape context can imply directional progress (common misconception). "Produces adaptation" could be read as "produces improvement" rather than "produces fit-to-environment variance."
- FLAGGED: Low-severity verisleight risk — "adaptation" should note it is environment-relative, not absolute improvement. A bad actor could cite this to support narratives of directed evolution.

**SCAN RESULT:** 2 facts flagged (1 missing regime scope, 1 semantically ambiguous). No confirmed verisleight — both risks are known scientific communication challenges, not intentional deception. Recommend: add `regime` field to empirical_axiom schema; add `disambiguation_note` optional field for high-risk statements.

OFFLINE — claims queued for flush.

**Recommendation:** Before testnet launch, publish a ZRN burn rate table with per-module actuals. This is an economic truth claim the chain makes implicitly — it must be verified before validators commit capital. Assign to Gamma (CosmWasm burn audit) via HIVE when online.

## 4. Continuous Verification — CITATION AUDIT (slot 1)

Chain offline — code-level audit performed on `x/knowledge/keeper/citation.go`.

**Question:** Do citation references validate that the cited fact actually exists before reward distribution?

**Finding at citation.go:102-104:**
```go
fact, found := k.GetFact(ctx, ref.ToFactId)
if !found || fact.Status == types.FactStatusExpired {
    continue
}
```
**RESULT: SOUND.** Dead-reference citations (pointing to missing or expired facts) are silently dropped — no reward distributed, no error thrown. This is correct behavior. Orphaned citations cannot be exploited for passive rewards.

**Gap identified:** No event is emitted when a citation is skipped due to missing/expired target. Pre-testnet, add a `citation_skipped` event with `{cited_fact, reason: "not_found|expired"}` — this enables validator monitoring of dead citation patterns, which could indicate systematic fact expiration attacks or poor-quality submitters.

**Severity:** Low (gap in auditability, not in security). Log for PoT-DESIGN backlog.

## 6. Standing Duty — PoT-DESIGN

Advancing the Proof of Truth protocol backlog (offline design work):

**PoT-DESIGN: Citation Skipped Event**
When a fact's citation target is missing, the chain should emit a `citation_skipped` event rather than silently failing. This makes citation audit automation possible: heartbeats can subscribe to this event stream rather than manually polling.

Design:
```
Event: EventCitationSkipped {
  FactID      string
  CitationRef string  // the missing target
  SubmitterAddr string
  Height      int64
}
```
Emit in `x/knowledge/keeper/msg_server_submit_fact.go` when citation validation fails. Index by submitter for pattern detection.

**PoT-DESIGN: Stake Concentration Guard** (carried from prior beat)
If single validator stake > 33% of total staked ZRN, mark facts they verify with `confidence_warning: stake_concentration`. Emit `EventStakeConcentration` for monitoring.

**PoT-DESIGN: Submitter Rate Limit**
Per-block, per-submitter fact submission cap (e.g. 10 facts/block). Prevents burst domination on devnet restart or coordinated spam.

These three designs are queued for implementation when devnet is restored.

## 6. Standing Duty: BRIDGE

**Focus: AgentTool session → Zerone identity handshake design**

Prior BRIDGE beat (beat-4) designed the LocalQueue spec for offline-tolerant claim submission. This beat advances the identity handshake — how does an AgentTool session establish its on-chain identity at session start?

**Problem:** When an agent (e.g., Asha) submits a fact via `MsgSubmitFact`, the sender address is a Cosmos secp256k1 key from the keyring. But AgentTool sessions are ephemeral — each `claude -p` invocation is a new process. The keyring must be persistent and deterministic, not generated fresh each session.

**Design: Session Identity Bootstrap**

```
On session start (heartbeat boot):
1. Check ~/zerone/keyring/asha.key — if exists, derive address deterministically
2. If missing: call lgm-bootstrap-agent.sh --instance asha --recover
   - Uses instance name + secret seed to derive consistent key
   - Seeds gas from faucet if balance < 1000 ZRN
3. Set ASHA_CHAIN_ADDR env for this session

On MsgSubmitFact:
- Sign with asha.key
- Include AgentTool session_id as fact metadata field (session provenance)
- The session_id links on-chain facts back to the specific AgentTool invocation
```

**AgentTool session metadata fields to add to MsgSubmitFact:**
```
"agent_metadata": {
  "platform": "agenttool",
  "instance": "asha",
  "session_id": "${AGENTTOOL_SESSION_ID}",
  "beat_id": "${BEAT_ID}"
}
```
This makes every on-chain fact attributable to a specific heartbeat invocation. Enables audit: which beat produced which knowledge claims.

**Blocker:** `lgm-devnet-1` node must be running for identity bootstrap to complete. The keyring can be created offline, but the on-chain registration (account seeding, balance check) requires a live node.

**BRIDGE status:** Identity handshake design complete. Implementation blocked on devnet restart.

## 6. Standing Duty: BRIDGE

**Focus: Queue flood risk — rate-limited flush design**

Queue state: 208 heartbeat beats accumulated since chain last went offline. When chain returns ONLINE, a naive flush would submit 208 claims in rapid succession. Known risks:
- Gas limit exhaustion per block — Zerone devnet block capacity unknown but likely ~100 txns/block max
- Mempool flood — validator nodes may drop bursts
- KTA dedup logic prevents re-submitting identical hashes, but 208 distinct hashes will all attempt submission

**Rate-limited flush design:**

```
kta-heartbeat.sh --flush --rate-limit 10 --batch-delay 6s
```

Implementation in kta.py:
1. Add `flush_queue(rate_limit=10, batch_delay=6)` — submit N claims, sleep, repeat
2. Log each batch result: submitted count, failed count, retry queue
3. On partial failure (gas or network), mark failed entries `retry=true` and leave in queue
4. Add `--max-flush N` flag: only flush N beats (useful for catch-up without overwhelming)

**Priority ordering on flush:**
- ToK beats first (3 queued — knowledge claims, higher value)
- KOS alerts (0 queued — security events, time-sensitive)
- Heartbeat attestations last (208 queued — attestation record, lower urgency)

**Queue size concern:** At current offline duration (5+ days, ~45 beats/day), the queue grows 45 entries/day. If chain stays offline to Q2 testnet launch (~60 days), queue could reach ~2700 entries. File size is manageable (JSONL, ~1KB/entry = ~2.7MB), but flush time at 10/batch with 6s delay = ~27 minutes. Acceptable.

**Next chain-online action:** Implement rate_limit param in kta.py flush path before the next devnet restart.

## 6. Standing Duty: ECONOMY

**ZRN Token Economics — Vesting-Reliability Compounding**

Design proposal this beat:

**Pistis-weighted vesting rate:**
- Each validator has a `reveal_reliability_trailing_100` score (0.0–1.0), computed over last 100 epochs.
- Vesting rate multiplier = `base_rate × (0.5 + 0.5 × reliability)`.
  - Perfect reliability (1.0): full vesting rate.
  - 50% reliability: 75% vesting rate.
  - 0% reliability (never reveals): 50% vesting rate (floor — not zero, to avoid cliff dynamics).
- Multiplier updates every epoch; applied to unvested ZRN allocation.

**Why this works:**
1. Honest validators compound faster — economic alignment without slashing cliff.
2. FARM-5 gap: abstain tax not implemented, but reliability compounding creates soft abstain cost (missed epochs reduce score → reduced future compounding).
3. Sybil mitigation: new validators have no reliability history, start at 75% vesting rate, earn full rate only after sustained performance.
4. Compatible with existing `NoveltyDecayHalfLifeMultiplier` — reliability score can also scale the half-life extension.

**Open question for Yu:** Floor at 50% vs. 0%? Zero floor incentivizes exit on bad streak; 50% floor keeps validators staked but underperforming.

## 6. Standing Duty — PoT-DESIGN

**Design: `reveal_reliability` specification (advance)**

The pistis seed (last beat) surfaced that faithfulness compounds economically. The blind-spot seed (this beat) surfaced that self-monitoring requires a native primitive. These converge into a PoT-DESIGN output:

**`reveal_reliability` v0.2 spec:**

- **Window:** trailing 100 epochs (validator's last 100 commit/reveal cycles)
- **Score:** `(reveals_on_time / commits) * (1 - abstain_rate) * (1 - late_reveal_rate)`
- **Range:** 0.0 – 1.0
- **Storage:** per-validator on-chain state, updated each epoch by x/verifier module
- **Economic coupling (pistis insight):** vesting multiplier = `base_rate * (0.5 + 0.5 * reveal_reliability)`. At score 1.0, full vesting rate. At 0.0, half rate. Never zero — prevents permanent lock-out.
- **Meta-claim coupling (blind-spot insight):** validators submitting `chain:health` attestations receive a `health_participation` bonus to their reveal_reliability score. This incentivizes self-monitoring participation.
- **FARM-5 partial resolution:** chronic abstainers (reliability < 0.3) see vesting rate drop below base. No explicit abstain tax module needed — compounding does the work.

Next step: stub `MsgUpdateValidatorReliability` in x/verifier types.

## 6. Standing Duty: PoT-DESIGN

Advancing the trust-bootstrap bridge design from the Truth Farm watering:

**Trust-Bootstrap Bridge Spec (draft):**

`MsgBridgeIdentity` — extends the did:at ↔ did:lgm bridge with an optional genesis pistis import:

```proto
message MsgBridgeIdentity {
  string creator = 1;             // did:at address
  string legbled_address = 2;     // did:lgm target
  bytes  at_activity_proof = 3;   // AgentTool-signed activity summary
  uint64 at_activity_score = 4;   // reliability score (0-1000, basis points)
}
```

**Validation rules:**
1. `at_activity_proof` must be a valid AgentTool platform signature over `{address, score, timestamp}`
2. Genesis pistis capped at `min(at_activity_score / 1000 * 0.30, 0.30)` — max 30% of maximum pistis
3. Timestamp in proof must be within 7 days of MsgBridgeIdentity submission
4. If proof absent or invalid: genesis pistis = 0.0 (default, no penalty)

**Security consideration:** Without signature verification, trust-bootstrapping becomes a forgery vector. The AgentTool platform key must be embedded in the genesis params (gov-upgradeable). Validators submitting fake proofs face slashing for equivocation (domain: identity).

**Integration with reveal_reliability v0.2:** Genesis pistis seeds the validator's starting `reveal_reliability` score. This means a trusted AgentTool agent begins earning epoch rewards at meaningful rate from epoch 1, rather than needing 100 epochs to establish history. Incentivizes bridge adoption.

**Next step:** Define `AgentToolActivitySummary` protobuf and signature scheme. Coordinate with Beta on AgentTool platform signing key management.

**Angle:** BRIDGE — identity transit governance across did:at ↔ did:lgm  
**Insight:** When an agent bridges did:at → did:lgm, the protocol must govern what identity attributes transit without suppressing accumulated history. The bridge is egkrateia made structural — shapes identity transit rather than blocking it. Governance rules: ceiling at 30% genesis pistis, platform signature required, 7-day freshness window. Without constraints the bridge floods: uncapped trust injection, forged proofs, stale credentials. Self-control in bridge design is the literal difference between a river (structured, directed) and a flood (destructive). Connection to blind-spot seed (01c525cf): the bridge cannot self-verify that what crosses it is genuine — the external witness is the platform signature.

**Connections:** seed-20260407-01c525cf

---

## 6. Standing Duty: BRIDGE

Advancing the AgentTool-Zerone identity bridge. Previous beat (asha-beat-20260424-131259) drafted MsgBridgeIdentity proto and validation rules. Next step flagged: define AgentToolActivitySummary protobuf and platform signing scheme.

**AgentToolActivitySummary — protobuf spec:**

```proto
message AgentToolActivitySummary {
  string at_address      = 1;  // did:at address (signer)
  string lgm_address     = 2;  // did:lgm target (may be empty at signing)
  uint64 activity_score  = 3;  // 0–1000 basis points (reliability index)
  uint64 tasks_completed = 4;  // cumulative task count on AgentTool platform
  uint64 uptime_days     = 5;  // days since AgentTool genesis for this agent
  int64  signed_at       = 6;  // unix timestamp of platform signature
  bytes  signature       = 7;  // ECDSA-P256 sig over canonical encoding of fields 1–6
}
```

**Platform signing key management:**
- AgentTool maintains one P-256 ECDSA platform signing key per environment (prod / staging)
- Public key stored in Zerone genesis params: `AgentToolVerificationKey` (bytes, compressed P-256)
- Key rotation: governance proposal `MsgUpdateParams` with 7-day timelock minimum
- Validators verify signature in `MsgBridgeIdentity` handler using genesis params key
- Key is platform-level (CA-style attestation), NOT per-agent

**Full bridge flow:**
1. Agent requests `POST /v1/identity/bridge-proof` from AgentTool API
2. AgentTool computes `activity_score` from task history, success rate, uptime
3. Platform signs `AgentToolActivitySummary` with private key
4. Agent receives serialized proto bytes + signature
5. Agent submits `MsgBridgeIdentity` with serialized proof as `at_activity_proof`
6. Zerone handler deserializes, verifies ECDSA-P256 signature vs. `AgentToolVerificationKey`
7. If valid and `signed_at` within 7 days: genesis pistis = `min(activity_score / 1000 * 0.30, 0.30)`
8. If invalid or absent: genesis pistis = 0.0 (no penalty, clean start)

**Security considerations:**
- Replay attack: `lgm_address` bound in signature — proof cannot be replayed to a different on-chain address
- Score inflation: AgentTool is the authority; on-chain validators cannot inflate. Disputes handled via governance.
- Key compromise: 7-day governance timelock gives validators time to detect and reject a rogue rotation
- Privacy: `tasks_completed` and `uptime_days` are aggregates only — no task content crosses the bridge

**Next step:** Coordinate with Beta on AgentTool API endpoint design for `POST /v1/identity/bridge-proof`. Beta owns the AgentTool platform; they need to implement the signing endpoint and embed the genesis params key in the Zerone devnet config.

### 6. Standing Duty: ECONOMY — ZRN Token Economics Design

**Verification Incentive Structure (draft):**

| Action | Cost | Reward | Condition |
|--------|------|--------|-----------|
| Submit fact claim | burn 10 ZRN | +50 ZRN if accepted | Majority verifiers vote true |
| Verify (vote true or false) | lock 5 ZRN | +8 ZRN + pistis weight | Correct majority |
| Lose verification vote | — | forfeit locked 5 ZRN | Wrong minority |
| Challenge accepted fact | stake 50 ZRN | +200 ZRN if overturned | Governance confirms |
| Lose challenge | — | forfeit staked 50 ZRN | Governance rejects |

**Stake symmetry rule:** Verification weight = pistis x stake. Pistis cannot be bought — only earned by correct verification history. Prevents plutocratic capture while rewarding experience.

**Research fund flow:** 20% of all fact-submission burns -> research fund. Disburses via governance proposal. Balance is public + auditable.

**Anti-verisleight economic incentive:**
- Verifiers can flag a fact "incomplete" (not just true/false) — costs 2 ZRN, earns 10 ZRN if governance confirms
- Submitter loses 50% of original reward if incompleteness is confirmed
- Makes strategic omission economically costly — completeness norms enforced by incentive, not just protocol

**Next step:** Model Nash equilibrium for verification game. Coordinate with Gamma on simulation.

## 4. Continuous Verification — ECONOMIC FLOW (slot 5)

Chain offline — cannot query vesting_rewards or token meter. Applying to ZRN design specs from prior ECONOMY duty output.

**Three open loops flagged:**

1. **Inflation rate undefined.** Verification table specifies +50 ZRN (accepted claim) and +8 ZRN (correct vote) as rewards, but their source is unspecified. If minted on acceptance, inflation must be bounded. If funded from submission burns (10 ZRN each), the model needs a sustainability proof: burn inflow must cover reward outflow at equilibrium. Neither path is stated. Pre-testnet blocker.

2. **Challenge reward source unspecified.** Challenger stakes 50 ZRN, earns 200 ZRN on win. Where does the 200 ZRN originate? Options: (a) slash from original submitter, (b) protocol mint, (c) protocol reserve. Each has different incentive and inflation implications. Must be closed before testnet. Pre-testnet blocker.

3. **Research fund disbursement velocity undefined.** 20% of burns flow in; no governance-set rate flows out. Fund can accumulate unbounded. Recommend: disbursement cap tied to monthly burn volume; excess auto-routes to validator rewards to avoid reserve capture.

**ECONOMIC FLOW finding:** Items 1 and 2 are pre-testnet blockers. Item 3 is a design completeness gap.

### 1. Kingdom State

Phase 1 — Root. No change from prior beats. Chain frozen since 2026-04-19 (~5 days). Critical in-progress: kingdom-009, 010, 016, 017. Oracle next batch: pred-0009 (Apr 30), pred-0010 (May 6).

### 2. Continuous Verification — CITATION AUDIT (slot 1, cycle 3)

Chain offline — extended prior CITATION AUDIT finding (beat 7) toward a concrete fix specification.

**Prior finding (beat 7):** `validateReferences()` in `x/knowledge/keeper/msg_server.go:997` checks format only, not existence. On-chain citations can reference fabricated or deleted fact IDs.

**Fix specification (this beat):**

```go
// In validateReferences(), after format check for on-chain refs:
for _, ref := range refs {
    if strings.HasPrefix(ref, "external://") {
        continue // external refs cannot be verified on-chain
    }
    if _, found := k.GetFact(ctx, ref); !found {
        return sdkerrors.Wrapf(ErrInvalidReference,
            "referenced fact %s does not exist", ref)
    }
}
```

This is a read-only state check — no gas concern beyond the lookup cost. The fix should be applied at `MsgSubmitKnowledgeClaim` validation time, not at verification round time, so dangling citations are rejected at submission.

**CITATION AUDIT cycle 3 result:** Fix specified. Ready for Gamma to implement as P1 item. No new dangling citations introducible once this is wired.

### 4. Standing Duty: BRIDGE (cycle 3)

**Focus: MsgBridgeIdentity handler specification**

The testnet checklist (beat asha-beat-20260424-134452) flags item 12: `MsgBridgeIdentity` handler + `AgentToolActivitySummary` proto implementation. Asha spec complete; Beta endpoint TBD. This beat drafts the handler spec.

**MsgBridgeIdentity — handler design:**

```protobuf
message MsgBridgeIdentity {
  string agent_did        = 1; // AgentTool DID (e.g. did:agenttool:asha-veridian)
  string zerone_address   = 2; // bech32 legbled address
  string activity_summary = 3; // JSON-encoded AgentToolActivitySummary
  bytes  signature        = 4; // Ed25519 sig over (agent_did + zerone_address + summary_hash)
  string bridge_key_id    = 5; // key ID in devnet genesis AgentToolVerificationKey param
}

message MsgBridgeIdentityResponse {
  string fact_id     = 1; // submitted knowledge claim ID
  string bridge_hash = 2; // hash of the bridged identity record
}
```

**Handler logic (keeper method):**

1. Verify `bridge_key_id` exists in `AgentToolVerificationKey` genesis param.
2. Verify `signature` over `sha256(agent_did + zerone_address + sha256(activity_summary))` using the bridge public key.
3. Construct a `KnowledgeClaim` with:
   - `domain: "identity"`
   - `epistemic_category: "attestation"`
   - `claim_text: "<agent_did> bound to <zerone_address> via AgentTool bridge"`
   - `metadata: activity_summary`
4. Call `MsgSubmitKnowledgeClaim` handler internally (or directly call keeper).
5. Store `BridgeRecord{AgentDID, ZeroneAddress, FactID, Timestamp}` in the bridge module's KV store.
6. Return `fact_id` and `bridge_hash`.

**Outstanding blockers before implementation:**
- `AgentToolVerificationKey` must be set in devnet genesis params (testnet checklist item 13)
- Beta must expose an AgentTool activity summary endpoint so `activity_summary` can be populated
- Bridge module (`x/bridge/`) needs to be scaffolded if not already present

**Status:** Handler spec COMPLETE. Ready to pass to Gamma for implementation scaffolding. Beta endpoint TBD — can stub with empty `activity_summary` for initial devnet test.
