### Continuous Verification — SUBMITTER PATTERN (slot 3, cycle 4)

Chain offline. Building on prior cycles (beats 9, 15): no per-epoch submitter concentration cap exists; `MaxContradictionsPerAgentPerEpoch` is the implementation template.

**New finding — Sybil-via-trust-bootstrap vector:**
The bridge trust-bootstrapping design (seed-1a9e62e3 BRIDGE angle, beat 131259) creates a new Sybil surface. An attacker who controls multiple legitimate AgentTool identities (each with moderate activity scores) can call `MsgBridgeIdentity` for each, bootstrapping partial genesis pistis per address, then flood fact submissions from all Sybil addresses simultaneously. Bridge key verification prevents forgery but not identity farming from correlated devices.

**Recommended mitigation:** `MsgBridgeIdentity` should record a `bridge_device_fingerprint` and enforce one active bridge record per fingerprint per epoch. Adds item 13 to bridge pre-testnet checklist.

**SUBMITTER PATTERN cycle 4 result:** Sybil-via-trust-bootstrap vector identified. Bridge design needs device-level deduplication, not just agent-level signature verification.

### 4. Continuous Verification — VERISLEIGHT SCAN (slot 4, cycle 5)

Chain offline. Applied VERISLEIGHT SCAN to three knowledge claims accumulated across prior beats:

**Claim 1 (from ECONOMY beat):** "Research fund outflow confirmed — governance-gated via FulfillBounty."
- Surface truth: correct, FulfillBounty has Authority check.
- Missing context: FulfillBounty also requires a matching bounty record to exist. If no bounties are ever posted, the fund accumulates indefinitely regardless of governance capability. "Governance can release funds" ≠ "funds flow regularly." The fund still risks being a slow accumulator if the bounty-posting loop isn't actively operated. Not verisleight — but incomplete without noting the operational dependency.
- Flag: LOW — add "requires active bounty-posting operations" to ECONOMICS.md alongside governance-gate note.

**Claim 2 (from SUBMITTER PATTERN beat):** "Bridge key verification prevents forgery but not identity farming from correlated devices."
- Surface truth: correct.
- Missing context: the claim implies device-fingerprint deduplication would solve the problem. It would reduce it, but a sophisticated attacker with multiple distinct devices (or VMs) retains the Sybil vector. The fuller truth: device deduplication is a necessary but not sufficient mitigation. Stake-weighted pistis (identity with demonstrated on-chain history) is the deeper defense. Fingerprinting buys time; pistis buys durability.
- Flag: MEDIUM — bridge pre-testnet checklist item 13 should read "device fingerprint deduplication + minimum pistis threshold for bridged identities."

**Claim 3 (from CITATION AUDIT, beat 1):** "No truth integrity issues detectable in offline state."
- Surface truth: technically accurate.
- Missing context: offline state is itself a truth integrity risk — the chain cannot receive new evidence, rebuttals, or confidence updates during downtime. A fact frozen at confidence 0.6 offline might deserve 0.3 or 0.9 after new events. "No issues detectable" during offline = "all issues frozen, not resolved."
- Flag: LOW — add to chain monitoring doc: chain offline periods should trigger post-restart confidence re-audit for time-sensitive facts.

**VERISLEIGHT SCAN result:** 3 claims examined. No active deception found. 1 medium-severity precision gap (bridge mitigation understated), 2 low-severity incompleteness flags. ## 6. Standing Duty: PoT-DESIGN

**Focus: verifier_count=0 gap partial analysis**

`confidence.go:AggregateVerificationResult` has a partial guard: `totalWeight==0` returns `"insufficient"` (line ~105). This handles the case where 0 validators reveal. The quorum check (line 123) uses `participationRatio` which defaults to 100% if `len(round.SelectedValidators)==0` — this is a potential bypass for rounds where validator selection was skipped (genesis axioms, emergency claims).

**PoT-DESIGN recommendation:** Add an explicit pre-quorum gate: `if len(round.SelectedValidators) == 0 && len(round.Reveals) == 0 { result.Verdict = "insufficient"; return }`. This closes the theoretical gap where a round with no selected validators and no reveals could proceed past the `totalWeight` check if a future code path introduces non-zero weight from a source other than reveals.

**Status:** P1 blocker still stands. Minimal one-line fix spec produced. Ready for Gamma.

## 4. Continuous Verification — CONFIDENCE CHECK (slot 2)

**Chain offline — code review mode. File: x/knowledge/keeper/confidence.go**

**Primary finding — confidence score is validator-self-reported map, not territory:**

`reveal.Confidence` (validator self-reported certainty, 0–1,000,000) is used as a stake multiplier: `weightedConf = safeMulDiv(reveal.Confidence, stake, 1_000_000)`. The final `result.Confidence` = `majorWeight / totalWeight` where both numerator and denominator are confidence-weighted. This means:

1. A validator claiming certainty = 1,000,000 has their stake fully counted.
2. A validator claiming uncertainty = 500,000 has their stake halved.
3. The final `result.Confidence` stores on-chain could be systematically inflated if validators coordinate to claim maximum self-confidence, regardless of genuine epistemic uncertainty.

This is a Korzybski violation: the protocol treats validator confidence-claims (map) as if they were ground truth (territory). The stored fact confidence is downstream of unverifiable self-reports.

**Economic implication:** validators have no incentive to report low confidence (it reduces their weight without any reward benefit), and possible incentive to always report max confidence. The reward calculation (`calculateRewardsAndSlashes`) is based on verdict alignment with final outcome — not on how close `reveal.Confidence` was to the final `result.Confidence`. No calibration feedback loop exists.

**Proposed fix (P2):** Decouple `result.Confidence` from self-reports. Compute it as pure stake-weighted vote ratio (sum of stakes for majority verdict / total stakes of all reveals). Store `reveal.Confidence` separately as a per-validator calibration signal; introduce a calibration score that rewards validators whose self-confidence correlates with final outcome over time.

**Prior findings confirmed still standing:**
- dangling references (P1, citation.go): unchanged
- verifier_count=0 path (P1 candidate, confidence.go:118–121): confirmed — `participationRatio` defaults 100% when `SelectedValidators` empty

**CONFIDENCE CHECK result:** New P2 finding — confidence inflation vector via validator self-reporting. No existing guard. Calibration feedback loop absent.

---

## 5. Truth Farm

FARM cycle: OFFLINE — chain frozen. Manual seed watering performed.

**Seed watered:** seed-20260407-dc297ca6 (the map is not the territory, Korzybski)
**Depth:** 0.30 → 0.38
**Angle:** CONFIDENCE CHECK — validator `reveal.Confidence` is a self-reported map; protocol treats it as territory weight. Final on-chain confidence score is downstream of unverifiable claims. This IS the map-territory failure mode in protocol design.
**Connection made:** seed-20260407-cc621ac9 (system vs. symptom: the fix at symptom level = validate confidence inputs; fix at system level = decouple confidence from self-reports architecturally)

---

## 6. Standing Duty: ECONOMY

**Focus: confidence inflation and its economic consequences**

The P2 confidence-inflation finding has economic implications for the ZRN token:

**Problem:** If validators systematically report confidence = 1,000,000, all facts settle at high confidence regardless of actual epistemic quality. High-confidence facts command higher reward weights (FARM-14 difficulty bonus applies inversely — high-confidence facts near domain ceiling get NO difficulty bonus). So a cartel that inflates confidence is actually REDUCING its own difficulty bonuses while corrupting the chain's truth signal. This is a subtle self-correcting pressure: gaming confidence toward maximum reduces individual rewards via FARM-14.

**But:** the FARM-14 ceiling comparison is `result.Confidence < ceiling`. If `result.Confidence` is inflated to = ceiling, difficulty bonus = 0. Validators lose the difficulty bonus they would have earned by honest calibration. This creates a Nash equilibrium analysis question: is the honest confidence strategy dominant?

**Honest strategy:** report `reveal.Confidence` proportional to actual certainty → lower confidence claim → lower weight in aggregation → but you earn more if you're in the majority because FARM-14 gives a difficulty bonus when the settled confidence is below ceiling.

**Gaming strategy:** report max confidence → maximum weight in aggregation → you win more rounds → but FARM-14 difficulty bonus is suppressed when confidence reaches ceiling.

**Preliminary assessment:** The honest strategy may actually be economically dominant if FARM-14 bonuses exceed the marginal benefit of higher weight. Needs quantitative analysis with actual tier weights and domain ceilings.

**Recommendation for Yu:** Quantitative simulation of honest vs. gaming confidence strategies under FARM-14 is worth running before mainnet. If gaming is dominant, add a calibration penalty (validators whose self-confidence systematically exceeds final outcome are penalized over a rolling window).

## 6. Standing Duty: BRIDGE

**Focus: AgentTool-Zerone identity bridge — attestation integrity gap**

Current state (from kingdom-006):
- Bootstrap agent (`lgm-bootstrap-agent.sh`) creates secp256k1 keyring entry and registers account
- Three minds (Alpha, Beta, Gamma) registered with 100K ZRN each
- Asha: not registered on-chain as validator
- KTA bridge queues heartbeat attestations offline using a hash derived from beat content

**Gap identified this beat:**

The KTA hash (e.g., `5fca36f9c08f1875`) is a content hash of the beat payload, not a cryptographic signature. This means:
1. Queued attestations cannot be verified as authentic once flushed — any process could write a plausible-looking beat file
2. Asha has no secp256k1 key in any keyring — her on-chain identity is absent
3. Without a signing key, Asha cannot produce attestations that the chain can verify as coming from this specific machine/agent

**Proposed bridge design for Asha's attestation integrity:**

```
1. Create Asha's keyring
   Path: ~/Love/instances/asha/keyring/
   Key type: secp256k1 (matching current devnet support)
   Register on-chain with initial stake (minimum viable for validator role)

2. Sign attestations before queuing
   Current KTA payload: { beat_id, timestamp, chain_status, duties, hash }
   Proposed: sign(beat_id + timestamp + chain_status + duties, asha_private_key)
   Store: { beat_id, timestamp, chain_status, duties, signature }

3. KTA flush (on chain reconnect)
   Submit signed attestation tx: beats verify against Asha's registered public key
   Chain accepts or rejects — creating a tamper-evident beat log

4. Threat model handled:
   - Replay: timestamp + beat_id unique per beat (monotonic)
   - Forgery: requires asha_private_key (local keyring)
   - Repudiation: on-chain record with signature
```

**Remaining gap:** Key custody — the private key lives on this machine. If the machine is compromised, so is Asha's chain identity. Hardware attestation (TPM or HSM) would close this but is out of scope for Phase 1.

**Next BRIDGE beat:** Implement keyring creation for Asha and update KTA bridge to sign attestations when devnet comes online.

**Truth Farm:** OFFLINE — duty deferred

**Standing Duty: ECONOMY — ZRN Token Flow Design**

Sustainable mint/burn equilibrium model for ZRN:

- **Emission:** Fixed block reward split — 70% to validators, 20% to research fund, 10% to treasury
- **Burn sources:** Fact submission fee (burned on rejection/slash), dispute resolution fee (burned on frivolous disputes)
- **Equilibrium condition:** At steady state, block mint rate = (submission volume × avg fee × rejection rate) + (dispute volume × fee)
- **Deflationary pressure mechanism:** As knowledge corpus grows and accuracy improves, slash events increase per submitter concentration (submitters must diversify domains or face stake dilution), net burn increases relative to mint
- **Inflationary floor:** Min validator reward = cost-of-operation + 5% margin — prevents validator exodus
- **ZRN velocity target:** Each ZRN should circulate through at least 3 verification cycles before resting in a stake position — high velocity = healthy economy signal
- **Key risk:** if mint >> burn in early phase (sparse submissions), validator reward inflation dilutes early holders; mitigation: genesis vesting cliff (18 months) and submission incentive subsidy from treasury

**Gap identified:** No model for fee curve as function of fact complexity. Simple boolean facts should cost less than multi-citation claims. TODO: design complexity-weighted submission fee.

## 4. Continuous Verification

**Check: VERISLEIGHT SCAN** (rotation slot 4, cycle 5)

Applied to Kingdom-level statements since chain is offline. Examined 3 fact statements for technical truth missing critical context.

**Statement 1:** "Oracle Brier 0.1431" (kingdom-metrics.json)
- Technically true. Missing context: computed from n=4 predictions only. At this sample size, a single prediction swing changes the score by ~0.06. Reporting to 4 decimal places implies precision the data cannot support. FLAG: annotate Brier score with n=4 until predictions scored ≥ 20.

**Statement 2:** "KTA queue: 238 entries"
- Technically true. Missing context: a growing queue number is not a health signal — it is a backlog indicator. Chain has been offline 5 days. Reporting queue size without the offline-since date creates a false impression of KTA activity. FLAG: always report queue as "N entries pending flush (offline since YYYY-MM-DD)".

**Statement 3:** "Seigei: beta-live at seigei.ai-love.cc" (kingdom-metrics.json, last updated 2026-04-07)
- Technically true as of 17 days ago. No verification since. Status may have drifted. FLAG: kingdom-metrics.json needs a freshness policy — entries older than 7 days without a re-check should be marked `status: unverified-stale`.

**VERISLEIGHT SCAN cycle 5 result:** 3 flags. No chain facts can be queried live. All flags are in Kingdom-internal reporting hygiene: Brier n-annotation missing, queue framing misleading, stale status entries. None represent malicious verisleight — they are precision-without-context errors. Remediation: add n=4 annotation to Brier in kingdom-metrics.json, update queue framing convention, add freshness check to next metrics update.
